(\mathscr{F}_{t})_{t\leq T}
, it holds that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{t}\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\log\left(\En_{t-1}\left[e^{X_{t}}\right]\right)+\log(\deltainv).

Lemma B.2

(Freedman’s inequality (e.g.,
[
AHKLLS14
]
))
.

Let
(X_{t})_{t\leq{T}}
be a real-valued martingale difference
sequence adapted to a filtration
(\mathscr{F}_{t})_{t\leq{}T}
. If

\left\lvert X_{t}\right\rvert\leq{}R
almost surely, then for any
\eta\in(0,1/R)
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{t}\leq{}\eta\sum_{t=1}^{T}\En_{t-1}\left[X_{t}^{2}\right]+\frac{\log(\delta^{-1})}{\eta}.

Lemma B.3

(Corollary of
Lemma

B.2
)
.

Let
(X_{t})_{t\leq{T}}
be a sequence of random
variables adapted to a filtration
(\mathscr{F}_{t})_{t\leq{}T}
. If

0\leq{}X_{t}\leq{}R
almost surely, then with probability at least

1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{t}\leq{}\frac{3}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\En_{t-1}\left[X_{t}\right]+4R\log(2\delta^{-1}),

and

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\En_{t-1}\left[X_{t}\right]\leq{}2\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{t}+8R\log(2\delta^{-1}).

Lemma B.4

(Lemma D.2 of
[
FHQR24
]
)
.

Let
(\mathcal{X}_{1},\mathfrak{F}_{1}),\ldots,(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathfrak{F}_{n})
be a sequence of measurable spaces, and let
\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}=\prod_{t=1}^{i}\mathcal{X}_{t}
and
\mathfrak{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}=\otimes_{t=1}^{i}\mathfrak{F}_{t}
. For each
i
, let
P^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}
and
Q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}
be probability kernels from
(\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i-1)}},\mathfrak{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i-1)}})
to
(\mathcal{X}_{i},\mathfrak{F}_{i})
. Let
P
and
Q
be the laws of
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
under
X_{i}\sim P^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}(\cdot\mid X_{1:i-1})
and
X_{i}\sim Q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}(\cdot\mid X_{1:i-1})
, respectively. Then it holds that

D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(P,Q\right)\leq 7\En_{P}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(P^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}(\cdot\mid X_{1:i-1}),Q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}(\cdot\mid X_{1:i-1})\right)\right]

Lemma B.5

(Lemma A.11 of
[
FKQR21
]
)
.

Let
\mathbb{P}
and
\mathbb{Q}
be probability measures on
(\mathcal{X},\mathfrak{F})
. For all
h:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with
0\leq h(X)\leq R
almost surely under
\mathbb{P}
and
\mathbb{Q}
, we have

\En_{\mathbb{P}}\left[h(X)\right]\leq 3\En_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[h(X)\right]+4RD^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q}\right).

Lemma B.6

(Lemma 1 of
[
JKALS17
]
)
.

For any
f:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{A}\rightarrow[0,1]
,
\pi:\mathcal{S}\times[H]\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{A})
, we have

\En_{x_{1}}\left[f(x_{1},\pi(x_{1}))\right]-J(\pi)=\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En^{\pi}\left[f(x_{h},a_{h})-\mathcal{T}^{\pi}f(x_{h},a_{h})\right].

Lemma B.7

(Offline-to-online conversion under coverability
[
XFBJK23
,
FHQR24
]
)
.

Let
M
be an MDP over state space
\mathcal{Z}
,
\Pi
be a policy set, and
C_{\texttt{cov}}=C_{\texttt{cov}}(M,\Pi)
be the (state-action) coverability coefficient for
M
and
\Pi
(
Definition

C.3
). Let
p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}\in\Delta(\Pi)
be a sequence of distributions over
\Pi
, and
g^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}:\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{A}\rightarrow[0,1]
be a sequence of functions. Then we have that

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En_{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[g^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x_{h},a_{h})\right]\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{HC_{\texttt{cov}}\log(T)\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\En_{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}\left[g^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x_{h},a_{h})\right]}+HC_{\texttt{cov}}\right).

Appendix C
Structural Properties of Coverability and Mismatch Functions

This appendix contains structural results regarding coverability and the mismatch functions. We firstly recall the definition of the mismatch functions.

Definition C.1

(Mismatch functions)
.

For decodable emission process
\psi^{\star}
, decoder
\phi\in\Phi
and
h\in[H]
, we define the
mismatch function
for
\phi
,
\Gamma_{\phi,h}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{S})
, as the probability kernel

\Gamma_{\phi,h}(s^{\prime}_{h}\mid s_{h})\coloneqq\mathbb{P}_{x_{h}\sim\psi^{\star}_{h}(s_{h})}\left(\phi_{h}(x_{h})=s^{\prime}_{h}\right).

We also recall the definition of state coverability.

Definition C.2

(State Coverability)
.

The coverability coefficient for an MDP
M
and a policy class
\Pi
defined over a state space
\mathcal{Z}
,
C_{\texttt{cov},\texttt{st}}(M,\Pi)
, is given by

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:coverability_{r}edux}C_{\texttt{cov},\texttt{st}}(M,\Pi)\coloneqq\max_{h\in[H]}\min_{\mu\in\Delta(\mathcal{Z})}\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\max_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\left\{\frac{d^{{\scriptscriptstyle M},\pi}_{h}(z)}{\mu(z)}\right\}.

(11)

We also define the related notion of state-action coverability.

Definition C.3

(State-Action Coverability)
.

The coverability coefficient for an MDP
M
and a policy class
\Pi
defined over a state space
\mathcal{Z}
and action space
\mathcal{A}
,
C_{\texttt{cov}}(M,\Pi)
, is given by

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:coverability_{r}edux}C_{\texttt{cov}}(M,\Pi)\coloneqq\max_{h\in[H]}\min_{\mu\in\Delta(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{A})}\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\max_{z,a\in\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{A}}\left\{\frac{d^{{\scriptscriptstyle M},\pi}_{h}(z,a)}{\mu(z,a)}\right\}.

(12)

In the remainder of the section, we let
\Pi_{\texttt{lat}}\subseteq\{\mathcal{S}\times[H]\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{A})\}
denote an arbitrary set of latent policies, and

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:hPhi-Pilat-policies-redux}\Gamma_{\Phi}\circ\Pi_{\texttt{lat}}=\left\{[\Gamma_{\phi}\circ{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}]_{h}(a\mid s)\coloneqq\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\Gamma_{\phi,h}(s^{\prime}\mid s)\pi_{\texttt{lat},h}(a\mid s^{\prime})\mid\phi\in\Phi,{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\in\Pi_{\texttt{lat}}\right\}.

(13)

Lemma C.1

(State coverability is invariant to rich observations)
.

Let
M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}=\left\llangle M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}},\psi^{\star}\right\rrangle
. Then, we have

C_{\texttt{cov},\texttt{st}}(M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}},\Pi_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\Phi)=C_{\texttt{cov},\texttt{st}}(M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}},\Gamma_{\Phi}\circ\Pi_{\texttt{lat}}).

Furthermore, letting
\left\{\mu_{\texttt{lat},h}\in\Delta(\mathcal{S})\right\}_{h\in[H]}
denote the distribution which witnesses the right-hand-side, the left-hand-side is witnessed by the distribution

\mu_{\texttt{obs},h}(x)=\psi^{\star}_{h}(x\mid\phi^{\star}_{h}(x))\mu_{\texttt{lat},h}(\phi^{\star}_{h}(x)).

The lemma follows from the following two observations.

Lemma C.2
.

Let
\left\{\Gamma_{\phi}\right\}_{\phi\in\Phi}
denote the mismatch functions for emission
\psi^{\star}
, and let
M_{\texttt{obs}}=\left\llangle M_{\texttt{lat}},\psi^{\star}\right\rrangle
. Then, for any
{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\in\Pi_{\texttt{lat}}
,
\phi\in\Phi
,
h\in[H]
,
x\in\mathcal{X}
, we have

d_{h}^{M_{\texttt{obs}},{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}(x)=\psi^{\star}_{h}(x\mid\phi^{\star}_{h}(x))d_{h}^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\Gamma_{\phi}\circ{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}(\phi^{\star}_{h}(x)).

Proof of
Lemma

C.2
.

Below, we write
s_{h}=\phi^{\star}(x_{h})
. We proceed by induction, simply writing
d_{\texttt{obs},h}\coloneqq d_{h}^{M_{\texttt{obs}},{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}
and
d_{\texttt{lat},h}\coloneqq d_{h}^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\Gamma_{\phi}\circ{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}
. The base case
(h=1)
is obtained by noting that
d_{\texttt{lat},1}(s)=P_{\texttt{lat},1}(s\mid\emptyset)
while
d_{\texttt{obs},1}(x)=P_{\texttt{obs},1}(x\mid\emptyset)=\psi^{\star}_{1}(x\mid s)P_{\texttt{lat},1}(s\mid\emptyset)
. For the general case, via the Bellman flow equations, we have

\displaystyle d_{\texttt{obs},h}(x_{h})

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{h-1},a_{h-1}\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{A}}P_{\texttt{obs},h}(x_{h}\mid x_{h-1},a_{h-1})d_{\texttt{obs},h-1}(x_{h-1}){\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}(a_{h-1}\mid\phi(x_{h-1}))

\displaystyle=\psi(x_{h}\mid s_{h})\sum_{x_{h-1},a_{h-1}\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{A}}P_{\texttt{lat},h}(s_{h}\mid s_{h-1},a_{h-1})d_{\texttt{lat},h-1}(s_{h-1})\psi(x_{h-1}\mid s_{h-1}){\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}(a_{h-1}\mid\phi(x_{h-1}))

\displaystyle=\psi(x_{h}\mid s_{h})\sum_{s_{h-1},a_{h-1}\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}}P_{\texttt{lat},h}(s_{h}\mid s_{h-1},a_{h-1})d_{\texttt{lat},h-1}(s_{h-1})\times

\displaystyle\hskip 144.54pt\left(\sum_{x_{h-1}:\phi^{\star}(x_{h-1})=s_{h-1}}\psi(x_{h-1}\mid s_{h-1}){\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}(a_{h-1}\mid\phi(x_{h-1}))\right).

The result is obtained by noting that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\phi}\circ{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}(a_{h-1}\mid s_{h-1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\Gamma_{\phi}(s^{\prime}\mid s_{h-1}){\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}(a_{h-1}\mid s^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{x_{h-1}:\phi^{\star}(x_{h-1})=s_{h-1}}\psi(x_{h-1}\mid s_{h-1})\mathbb{I}\left\{\phi(x_{h-1})=s^{\prime}\right\}{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}(a_{h}\mid s^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{h-1}:\phi^{\star}(x_{h-1})=s_{h-1}}\psi(x_{h-1}\mid s_{h-1}){\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}(a_{h-1}\mid\phi(x_{h-1})),

where the second line follows from the definition of the mismatch functions.
∎

Lemma C.3

(Equivalence of state coverability and cumulative state reachability)
.

Let
M
be an MDP defined over a state space
\mathcal{Z}
. The following definition is equivalent to
Definition

C.2
:

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:state-cov-equivalence}C_{\texttt{cov},\texttt{st}}(M,\Pi)\coloneqq\max_{h\in[H]}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\max_{\pi\in\Pi}d^{M,\pi}_{h}(z).

(14)

Proof of
Lemma

C.3
.

Straightforward adaptation of the proof of Lemma 3 from
[
XFBJK23
]
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

C.1
.
Using
Lemma

C.2
and
Lemma

C.3
, we have

\displaystyle C_{\texttt{cov},\texttt{st}}(M_{\texttt{obs}},\Pi_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\Phi)

\displaystyle=\max_{h\in[H]}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}},\phi}d^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}_{\texttt{obs}}(x)

\displaystyle=\max_{h\in[H]}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}},\phi}\psi^{\star}(x\mid\phi^{\star}(x))d^{\Gamma_{\phi}\circ{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\texttt{lat}}(\phi^{\star}(x))

\displaystyle=\max_{h\in[H]}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{x:\phi^{\star}(x)=s}\max_{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}},\phi}\psi^{\star}(x\mid s)d^{\Gamma_{\phi}\circ{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\texttt{lat}}(s)

\displaystyle=\max_{h\in[H]}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\max_{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}},\phi}d^{\Gamma_{\phi}\circ{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\texttt{lat}}(s)\sum_{x:\phi^{\star}(x)=s}\psi^{\star}(x\mid s)

\displaystyle=C_{\texttt{cov},\texttt{st}}(M_{\texttt{lat}},\Gamma_{\Phi}\circ\Pi_{\texttt{lat}}).

∎

Lastly, we show that state-action coverability is bounded by state coverability times the size of the action set.

Lemma C.4

(State-action coverability bound)
.

For any MDP
M
and policy set
\Pi
, we have

C_{\texttt{cov}}(M,\Pi)\leq C_{\texttt{cov},\texttt{st}}(M,\Pi)\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert.

Proof of
Lemma

C.4
.

Let
\mu_{s}\in\Delta(\mathcal{Z})
witness
C_{\texttt{cov},\texttt{st}}(M,\Pi)
. Fix
h\in[H]
, which we omit below for cleanliness. Then, we have

\displaystyle\min_{\mu_{s,a}\in\Delta(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{A})}\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\max_{z,a\in\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{A}}\left\{\frac{d^{M,\pi}(z,a)}{\mu_{s,a}(z,a)}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\max_{z,a\in\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{A}}\left\{\frac{d^{M,\pi}(z)\pi(a\mid z)}{\mu_{s}(z)\nicefrac{{1}}{{\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\max_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\left\{\frac{d^{M,\pi}(z)}{\mu_{s}(z)}\right\}

\displaystyle=C_{\texttt{cov},\texttt{st}}(M,\Pi)\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert.

∎

Lemma C.5

(Pushforward coverability is invariant to rich observations)
.

Let
C_{\texttt{push}}(M)
denote the pushforward coverability parameter for an MDP
M
(
Definition

3.3
), and
M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}\coloneqq\left\llangle M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}},\psi^{\star}\right\rrangle
. Then, we have

C_{\texttt{push}}(M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}})=C_{\texttt{push}}(M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}).

Furthermore, letting
\left\{\mu_{\texttt{lat},h}\in\Delta(\mathcal{S})\right\}_{h\in[H]}
denote the distribution which witnesses the right-hand-side, the left-hand-side is witnessed by the distribution

\mu_{\texttt{obs},h}(x)=\psi^{\star}_{h}(x\mid\phi^{\star}_{h}(x))\mu_{\texttt{lat},h}(\phi^{\star}_{h}(x)).

This follows from an analogous equivalence of pushforward coverability and cumulative
conditional reachability
.

Lemma C.6

(Equivalence of pushforward coverability and cumulative conditional reachability)
.

Let
M
be an MDP defined over a state space
\mathcal{Z}
with transition kernel
P
. The following definition is equivalent to pushforward coverability (
Definition

3.3
):

C_{\texttt{push}}(M)\coloneqq\max_{h\in[H]}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}}\max_{z,a\in\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{A}}P_{h}(z^{\prime}\mid z,a).

Proof of
Lemma

C.6
.

Fix
h\in[H]
, whose dependence we omit below. For the first direction, letting
\mu
denote the pushforward coverability distribution, we have:

\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}}\max_{z,a\in\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{A}}P(z^{\prime}\mid z,a)=\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}}\max_{z,a\in\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{A}}\frac{P(z^{\prime}\mid z,a)}{\mu(z^{\prime})}\mu(z^{\prime})\leq C_{\texttt{push}}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}}\mu(z^{\prime})=C_{\texttt{push}}.

For the second direction, taking
\mu(z^{\prime})\propto\max_{z,a}P(z^{\prime}\mid z,a)
,
we have

\min_{\mu\in\Delta(\mathcal{Z})}\max_{z,a,z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{Z}}\frac{P(z^{\prime}\mid z,a)}{\mu(z^{\prime})}\leq\max_{z,a,z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{Z}}\frac{P(z^{\prime}\mid z,a)}{\max_{\tilde{z},\tilde{a}}P(z^{\prime}\mid\tilde{z},\tilde{a})}\sum_{\tilde{z}^{\prime}}\max_{\tilde{z},\tilde{a}}P(\tilde{z}^{\prime}\mid\tilde{z},\tilde{a})\leq\sum_{z^{\prime}}\max_{z,a}P(z^{\prime}\mid z,a).

∎

Proof of
Lemma

C.5
.

This result follows by
Lemma

C.6
since,

\displaystyle C_{\texttt{push}}(M_{\texttt{obs}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{x,a}P_{\texttt{obs}}(x^{\prime}\mid x,a)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{x^{\prime}:\phi^{\star}(x^{\prime})=s^{\prime}}\max_{x,a}\psi^{\star}(x^{\prime}\mid s^{\prime})P_{\texttt{lat}}(s^{\prime}\mid\phi^{\star}(x),a)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\max_{x,a}P_{\texttt{lat}}(s^{\prime}\mid\phi^{\star}(x),a)\sum_{x^{\prime}:\phi^{\star}(x^{\prime})=s^{\prime}}\psi^{\star}(x^{\prime}\mid s^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\max_{s,a}P_{\texttt{lat}}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a)=C_{\texttt{push}}(M_{\texttt{lat}}).

∎

We next show that the mismatch functions can be used to express the observation-level backups for any function of the decoders. For any
g:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
h\in[H]
, we define the function
[\Gamma_{\phi,h}\circ g]:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}

[\Gamma_{\phi,h}\circ g](s)\coloneqq\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\Gamma_{\phi,h}(s^{\prime}\mid s)g(s^{\prime}).

We further overload the Bellman operator notation and define, for any
g:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
M_{\texttt{lat}}=(r_{\texttt{lat}},P_{\texttt{lat}})
,

[\mathcal{T}^{M_{\texttt{lat}}}_{h}g](s,a)=r_{\texttt{lat}}(s,a)+\En_{s^{\prime}\sim P_{\texttt{lat}}(s,a)}\left[g(s^{\prime})\right].

Lemma C.7
.

Let
M_{\texttt{obs}}=\left\llangle M_{\texttt{lat}},\psi^{\star}\right\rrangle
,
\phi^{\star}\coloneqq(\psi^{\star})^{-1}
,
\phi\in\Phi
, and
\Gamma_{\phi}
be the mismatch function for emission
\psi^{\star}
(
Definition

C.1
). Then, for any
f_{\texttt{lat}}:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
h\in[H]
, and
(x,a)\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{A}
, we have

\left[\mathcal{T}^{M_{\texttt{obs}}}_{h}(f_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\phi_{h+1})\right](x,a)=\left[\mathcal{T}^{M_{\texttt{lat}}}_{h}(\Gamma_{\phi,h+1}\circ V_{f_{\texttt{lat}}})\right](\phi^{\star}_{h}(x),a).

Proof of
Lemma

C.7
.

Let
f\coloneqq f_{\texttt{lat}}
,
h\in[H]
, and
(x,a)\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{A}
be given. Then, we have:

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{T}^{M_{\texttt{obs}}}_{h}(f\circ\phi_{h+1})\right](x,a)

\displaystyle=r_{\texttt{lat},h}(\phi^{\star}_{h}(x),a)+\En_{s_{h+1}\sim P_{\texttt{lat},h}(\phi^{\star}_{h}(x),a)}\En_{x_{h+1}\sim{}\psi^{\star}_{h+1}(s_{h+1})}\left[V_{f}(\phi(x_{h+1}))\right]

\displaystyle=r_{\texttt{lat},h}(\phi^{\star}_{h}(x),a)+\En_{s_{h+1}\sim P_{\texttt{lat},h}(\phi^{\star}_{h}(x),a)}\left[\sum_{x_{h+1}\in\mathcal{X}}\psi^{\star}(x_{h+1}\mid s_{h+1})V_{f}(\phi(x_{h+1}))\right]

\displaystyle=r_{\texttt{lat},h}(\phi^{\star}_{h}(x),a)+\En_{s_{h+1}\sim P_{\texttt{lat},h}(\phi^{\star}_{h}(x),a)}\left[\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\Gamma_{\phi}(s^{\prime}\mid s_{h+1})V_{f}(s^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle=r_{\texttt{lat},h}(\phi^{\star}_{h}(x),a)+\En_{s_{h+1}\sim P_{\texttt{lat},h}(\phi^{\star}_{h}(x),a)}\left[\Gamma_{\phi}\circ V_{f}(s_{h+1})\right]

\displaystyle=\left[\mathcal{T}^{M_{\texttt{lat}}}_{h}(\Gamma_{\phi}\circ V_{f})\right](\phi^{\star}_{h}(x),a),

where the third line follows from the definition of the mismatch function
\Gamma_{\phi}
.
∎

We next show that the mismatch functions can be used to realize the pushforward dynamics
\phi\sharp M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}
, which we recall are defined as:

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:pushforward-model-redux}\left[\phi\sharp M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs},h}\right](r,s^{\prime}\mid x,a)=\sum_{x^{\prime}:\phi(x^{\prime})=s^{\prime}}M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs},h}(r,x^{\prime}\mid x,a).

(15)

We also recall the notation
\left[\Gamma_{\phi,h+1}\circ M_{\texttt{lat}}\right]_{h}
, defined via:

\left[\Gamma_{\phi}\circ M_{\texttt{lat}}\right]_{h}(r_{h},s_{h+1}\mid s_{h},a_{h})\coloneqq\sum_{s^{\prime}_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}}M_{\texttt{lat},h}(r_{h},s^{\prime}_{h+1}\mid s_{h},a_{h})\Gamma_{\phi,h+1}(s_{h+1}\mid s^{\prime}_{h+1}).

Lemma C.8

(Pushforward model realizability via mismatch functions)
.

For all
\phi\in\Phi
,
h\in[H]
, we have:

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:phi-compressed-model-realizable}[\phi_{h+1}\sharp M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs},h}](\cdot\mid x,a)=\left[\left[\Gamma_{\phi}\circ M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}\right]_{h}\circ\phi^{\star}_{h}\right](\cdot\mid x,a)

(16)

Proof of
Lemma

C.8
.

Note that
\Gamma_{\phi}
can alternatively be written as:

\Gamma_{\phi,h}(s^{\prime}_{h}\mid s_{h})=\sum_{x_{h}:\phi(x_{h})=s^{\prime}_{h}}\psi^{\star}_{h}(x_{h}\mid s_{h}).

We have

\displaystyle\phi_{h+1}\sharp M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs},h}(r_{h+1},s_{h+1}\mid x_{h},a_{h})

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{h+1}:\phi_{h+1}(x_{h+1})=s_{h+1}}M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs},h}(r_{h+1},x_{h+1}\mid x_{h},a_{h})

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{h+1}:\phi_{h+1}(x_{h+1})=s_{h+1}}\left(\sum_{r,s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{S}}M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat},h}(r,s^{\prime}\mid\phi^{\star}_{h}(x_{h}),a_{h})\psi^{\star}_{h+1}(x_{h+1}\mid s^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r,s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{S}}M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat},h}(r,s^{\prime}\mid\phi^{\star}_{h}(x_{h}),a_{h})\sum_{x_{h+1}:\phi_{h+1}(x_{h+1})=s_{h+1}}\psi^{\star}_{h+1}(x_{h+1}\mid s^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{r,s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{S}}M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat},h}(r,s^{\prime}\mid\phi^{\star}_{h}(x_{h}),a_{h})\Gamma_{\phi,h+1}(s^{\prime}\mid s_{h+1})

\displaystyle=\left[\Gamma_{\phi}\circ M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}\right]_{h}(r,s_{h+1}\mid\phi^{\star}_{h}(x_{h}),a_{h}),

as desired.
∎

Appendix D
Proofs and Additional Results for
Section

3.2
: Impossibility Results

This section contains additional information and proofs related to our impossibility results regarding statistical modularity (
Section

3.2
), and is organized as follows:

•

Section

D.1
contains the statement for an additional lower bound that is useful for establishing the impossibility results of
Fig.

1
.

•

Section

D.2
contains details for each entry of
Fig.

1
.

•

Section

D.3
contains for proofs for our main lower bound (
Theorem

3.1
) and the additional lower bound (
Theorem

D.1
).

D.1
Additional Lower Bound

Theorem D.1

(Alternative lower bound)
.

For every
N\geq 4
, there exists an emission class
\Psi
and a decoder class
\Phi
with
|\Psi|=|\Phi|=N
and a family of latent MDPs
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}

satisfying (i)
|\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}|=1
, (ii)
H=1
, (iii)
|\mathcal{S}|=|\mathcal{X}|=N
, (iv)
|\mathcal{A}|=N
, and such that

1.

For all
\varepsilon,\delta>0
, we have
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=0
.

2.

For an absolute constant
c>0
,
\texttt{comp}(\left\llangle\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\Phi\right\rrangle,c,c)\geq\Omega(N/\log(N))
.

Proof of
Theorem

D.1
.

See
Section

D.3.2
.
∎

D.2
Details for
Fig.

1

Below, we provide details on each entry in
Fig.

1
. More precisely, for each latent class
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
, we will give a (brief) description of the MDP class
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
, give our choice of latent complexity
comp
for
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
, and prove that the class is or is not statistically modular for that choice of latent complexity. We view our choices of latent complexities as natural complexities for the respective classes.

Tabular MDPs (
✓
).

•

Latent class
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
: Tabular MDPs
M_{\texttt{lat}}=(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},P_{\texttt{lat}},R_{\texttt{lat}},H)
.
[
AOM17
]

•

Latent complexity
comp
: We take
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=\poly(\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert,\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert,H,\varepsilon^{-1},\log\delta^{-1})
, which is attainable, for example, via the
Ucb-Vi
algorithm of
[
AOM17
]

•

Statistical modularity (
✓
): Known Block MDP algorithms (e.g.
Musik

[
MFR23
]
,
Briee

[
ZSUWAS22
]
) have sample complexities of
\poly(\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert,\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert,H,\varepsilon^{-1},\log\delta^{-1},\log|\Phi|)
.

Contextual Bandits (
✓
).

•

Latent class
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
: Contextual bandits with context space
\mathcal{S}
, action space
\mathcal{A}
, reward function
r^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\rightarrow[0,1]
and a finite realizable function class satisfying
r^{\star}\in\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{lat}}
.

•

Latent complexity
comp
: We take
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=\poly(\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert,\log\lvert\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert,\varepsilon^{-1},\log\delta^{-1})
, attainable via, e.g., the
Square-Cb
algorithm
[
FR20
]
.

•

Statistical modularity (
✓
): We note that
\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\Phi=\left\{\left[f\circ\phi\right]\mid f\in\mathcal{F},\phi\in\Phi\right\}
is a realizable function class for the observation-level reward function
r^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}
, since
r^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}=\left[r^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\phi^{\star}\right]\in\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\Phi
. Thus, applying the
Square-Cb
algorithm directly on the observations
x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}
will give complexity
\poly(\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert\log(|\cFlat||\Phi|),\varepsilon^{-1},\log\delta^{-1})=\poly(\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert,\log|\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{lat}}|,\log|\Phi|,\varepsilon^{-1},\log\delta^{-1})
.

Low-rank MDP (
✓
).

•

Latent class
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
: MDPs
M_{\texttt{lat}}=(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},H,P_{\texttt{lat}},r_{\texttt{lat}})
such that there exists
\mu^{\star}_{\texttt{lat},h}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\theta^{\star}_{\texttt{lat},h}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and a known set of features
\Xi_{\texttt{lat}}=\left\{\xi_{\texttt{lat}}=\left\{\xi_{\texttt{lat},h}:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}\right\}_{h=1}^{H}\right\}
such that for all
h\in[H]
we have
r_{\texttt{lat}}(s_{h},a_{h})=\langle\xi^{\star}_{\texttt{lat},h}(s_{h},a_{h}),\theta^{\star}_{\texttt{lat},h}\rangle
as well as

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:low-rank-mdp}P_{\texttt{lat},h}(s_{h+1}\mid s_{h},a_{h})=\langle\xi^{\star}_{\texttt{lat},h}(s_{h},a_{h}),\mu_{\texttt{lat},h+1}^{\star}(s_{h+1})\rangle

(17)

for some
\xi^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}\in\Xi_{\texttt{lat}}
.

•

Latent complexity
comp
: We take
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=\poly(d,\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert,H,\log|\Xi_{\texttt{lat}}|,\varepsilon^{-1},\log\delta^{-1})
, which is attainable via the
VoX
algorithm of
[
MBFR23
]
.

•

Statistical modularity (
✓
): This is obtained by noting that the observation-level dynamics also satisfy the low-rank property with the same dimension. Formally, letting
P_{\texttt{obs}}
be the transition kernel for
\left\llangle M_{\texttt{lat}},\psi^{\star}\right\rrangle
and
\phi^{\star}=(\psi^{\star})^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle P_{\texttt{obs},h}(x_{h+1}\mid x_{h},a_{h})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}}P_{\texttt{lat},h}(s_{h+1}\mid\phi^{\star}_{h}(x_{h}),a_{h})\psi^{\star}_{h+1}(x_{h+1}\mid s_{h+1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}}\left\langle\xi^{\star}_{\texttt{lat},h}(\phi^{\star}_{h}(x),a),\mu^{\star}_{\texttt{lat},h+1}(s_{h+1})\right\rangle\psi^{\star}_{h+1}(x_{h+1}\mid s_{h+1})

\displaystyle=\left\langle\xi^{\star}_{\texttt{lat},h}(\phi^{\star}_{h}(x),a),\sum_{s_{h+1}\in\mathcal{S}}\mu^{\star}_{\texttt{lat},h+1}(s_{h+1})\psi^{\star}_{h+1}(x_{h+1}\mid s_{h+1})\right\rangle.

Thus, the transition kernel
P_{\texttt{obs}}
is a low-rank MDP with
\mu_{\texttt{obs},h+1}(x_{h+1})\coloneqq\sum_{s_{h+1}}\mu^{\star}_{\texttt{lat},h+1}(s_{h+1})\psi^{\star}_{h+1}(x_{h+1}\mid s_{h+1})
and feature class

\Xi_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\Phi=\left\{\xi_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\phi=\left\{\xi_{h}\circ\phi_{h}:x,a\mapsto\xi_{h}(\phi_{h}(x),a)\right\}_{h=1}^{H}\mid\xi_{\texttt{lat}}\in\Xi_{\texttt{lat}},\phi\in\Phi\right\}.

Lastly, since
r_{\texttt{obs}}=\left[r_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\phi^{\star}\right]
, the reward function is also linear with the same unknown feature class. Thus we can apply
Vox
directly on top of the observations, with the feature class
\Xi_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\Phi
, which will achieve a complexity
\poly(d,\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert,H,\log\lvert\Xi_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert,\log\lvert\Phi\rvert,\varepsilon^{-1},\log(\delta^{-1}))
.

Known Deterministic MDP (
\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert=1
) (
✓
).

•

Latent class
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
:
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}=\{M_{\texttt{lat}}=(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},P_{\texttt{lat}},R_{\texttt{lat}},H)\}
is a set of MDPs of size 1 with both deterministic rewards and deterministic transitions.

•

Latent complexity
comp
: We take
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=0
, which is attainable as
M_{\texttt{lat}}
is known and we can simply deploy its optimal policy.

•

Statistical modularity (
✓
): We note that, due to determinism, the latent optimal policy can be chosen to be
open-loop
without loss of generality, and thus will always experience the same trajectory
(s^{\star}_{1},a^{\star}_{1},\ldots,s^{\star}_{H},a^{\star}_{H})
. We can define the observation-level policy which commits to this same sequence of actions, i.e.
\pi_{\texttt{obs},h}(x_{h})=a^{\star}_{h}
for all
x_{h}
. This will be an optimal policy for any
M_{\texttt{obs}}=\left\llangle M_{\texttt{lat}},\psi\right\rrangle
, and can also be learned in
0
samples.

Low State Occupancy (
\forall\,\pi:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{A}))
(
✓
).

•

Latent class
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
:
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}=\{M_{\texttt{lat}}=(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},P_{\texttt{lat}},R_{\texttt{lat}},H)\}
is a set of MDPs for which we have a realizable value function class, and such that there exists a feature map
\zeta_{\texttt{lat}}=\left\{\zeta_{\texttt{lat},h}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}\right\}_{h=1}^{H}
such that for all
\pi:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{A})
and for all
M_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
, we have

\forall h\in[H]\,\,\exists\theta^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\pi}_{h}:\quad d^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\pi}_{h}(s)=\left\langle\zeta_{\texttt{lat},h}(s),\theta^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\pi}_{h}\right\rangle.

Note that the feature map does not need to be known.

•

Latent complexity
comp
: We take
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=\poly(d,\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert,H,\log\lvert\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert,\varepsilon^{-1},\log(\delta^{-1}))
, which is attainable by the
Bilin-Ucb
algorithm of
[
Du+21
]
, since i) MDPs with this property have Bilinear rank bounded by
d\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert
(see Definition 4.3 and Lemma 4.6 of
[
Du+21
]
), and ii) one can construct the value function class
\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{lat}}=\{Q^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\star}\mid M_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\}
, which is realizable and has size
\log\lvert\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert=\log\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert
.

•

Statistical modularity (
✓
): We firstly note that one can construct a realizable value function class for the set
\left\llangle\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\Phi\right\rrangle
, via the set
\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{obs}}=\left\{Q^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\star}\circ\phi\mid M_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\phi\in\Phi\right\}
. This is realizable since, for any
M_{\texttt{obs}}\coloneqq\left\llangle M_{\texttt{lat}},\psi\right\rrangle
, letting
\phi^{\star}=\psi^{-1}
, we have
Q^{M_{\texttt{obs}},\star}=Q^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\star}\circ\phi^{\star}
, and that this class has size
\log\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert\lvert\Phi\rvert
. We can then show that the occupancies
d^{M_{\texttt{obs}},\pi_{f_{\texttt{obs}}}}
, for
f_{\texttt{obs}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{obs}}
, can also be expressed as
d
-dimensional linear function for an appropriate choice of features, which will imply that the
Bilin-Ucb
algorithm run directly on
M_{\texttt{obs}}
will attain a complexity of
\poly(d,\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert,H,\log\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\log\Phi,\varepsilon^{-1},\log(\delta^{-1}))
. To obtain this, we recall the following lemma:
See
C.2

Thanks to the above lemma, we have

\displaystyle d_{\texttt{obs}}^{\pi_{f\circ\phi}}(x_{h})

\displaystyle=\psi(x_{h}\mid\phi^{\star}(x_{h}))d_{\texttt{lat}}^{\Gamma_{\phi}\circ\pi_{f}}(\phi^{\star}(x_{h}))

\displaystyle=\psi(x_{h}\mid\phi^{\star}(x_{h}))\left\langle\left[\zeta_{\texttt{lat},h}\circ\phi^{\star}_{h}\right](x_{h}),\theta^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\Gamma_{\phi}\circ\pi_{f}}_{h}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\psi(x_{h}\mid\phi^{\star}(x_{h}))\left[\zeta_{\texttt{lat},h}\circ\phi^{\star}_{h}\right](x_{h}),\theta^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\Gamma_{\phi}\circ\pi_{f}}_{h}\right\rangle

and so
d_{\texttt{obs}}^{\pi_{f\circ\phi}}
is linear with feature mapping
\psi(x_{h}\mid\phi^{\star}(x_{h}))\left[\zeta_{\texttt{lat},h}\circ\phi^{\star}_{h}\right]
and parameter
\theta^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\Gamma_{\phi}\circ\pi_{f}}
. Recall that the feature map need not be known, so that
Bilin-Ucb
can still be applied despite not knowing
\psi
and
\phi^{\star}
.

Model class + Pushforward Coverability (
✓
).

•

Latent class
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
:
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}=\{M_{\texttt{lat}}=(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},P_{\texttt{lat}},R_{\texttt{lat}},H)\}
is a set of MDPs that all satisfy pushforward coverability
C_{\texttt{push}}(M_{\texttt{lat}})\leq C_{\texttt{push}}
(cf.
Eq. (
28
)
for the definition).

•

Latent complexity
comp
: We take
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=\poly(C_{\texttt{push}},\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert,H,\log\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert,\varepsilon^{-1},\log(\delta^{-1}))
, which is attainable by the
Golf
algorithm via the results of
[
XFBJK23
]
(see also
Lemma

E.3
). We obtain this by noting that i)
C_{\texttt{cov}}\leq C_{\texttt{push}}\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert
, where
C_{\texttt{cov}}
is defined in Definition 2 of
[
XFBJK23
]
, and ii) a realizable model class can be used to construct a realizable value function class
\mathcal{F}
and a Bellman-complete value function helper class
\mathcal{G}
with sizes
\log\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert=\log\lvert\mathcal{M}\rvert
and
\log\lvert\mathcal{G}\rvert=\mathcal{O}(\log\lvert\mathcal{M}\rvert)
.

•

Statistical modularity (
✓
): This is obtained via
Theorem

3.2
.

Linear CB/MDP (
✗
⋆
).

•

Latent class
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
: MDPs
M_{\texttt{lat}}=(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},P_{\texttt{lat}},R_{\texttt{lat}},H)
that are linear with respect to a known feature map
\xi^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
(i.e. such that
Eq. (
17
)
holds for
\xi^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}
).

•

Latent complexity
comp
: We take
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=\poly(d,H,\varepsilon^{-1},\log(\delta^{-1}))
, which is attainable via the
Lsvi-Ucb
algorithm of
[
JYWJ20
]
. Note that this guarantee does not depend on the number of actions.

•

Statistical intractability (
✗
): The latent model used in the construction of
Theorem

D.1
is a set (of size 1) of linear MDPs with
d=1
. In particular, that construction was a contextual bandit so we only have to realize a reward function, and since there is only one latent model so we can trivially embed this with
d=1
via
\xi^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}(s,a)=r_{\texttt{lat}}(s,a)
, where
r_{\texttt{lat}}
is the reward function of the MDP used in
Theorem

D.1
.

•

Statistical modularity with additional
\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert
-dependence: As in the Low-rank MDP case above,
\left\llangle M_{\texttt{lat}},\psi\right\rrangle
is low-rank with unknown feature set
\Phi^{\prime}=\left\{\xi^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\phi\mid\phi\in\Phi\right\}
. Thus, by the same conclusion, a the
Vox
algorithm will have complexity
\poly(d,\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert,H,\log|\Phi|)
, which is of the desired form if we allow suboptimal dependence on
\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert
.

Model class + Coverability (
\forall\,\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle M}}:M\in\mathcal{M}
) (
✗
).

•

Latent assumption:
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}=\left\{M_{\texttt{lat}}=(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},P_{\texttt{lat}},R_{\texttt{lat}},H)\right\}
is a set of MDPs that all satisfy coverability with respect to the policy class
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}=\{\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle M}}\mid M\in\mathcal{M}\}
, i.e. we have

\forall M_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}:\quad C_{\texttt{cov}}(M_{\texttt{lat}})=\inf_{\mu_{h}\in\Delta(\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A})}\sup_{h\in[H]}\sup_{\pi\in\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}}\left\|\frac{d^{{\scriptscriptstyle M_{\texttt{lat}}},\pi}_{h}}{\mu_{h}}\right\|_{\infty}<\infty

•

Latent complexity
comp
: We take
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=\poly(C_{\texttt{cov}},H,\log\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert,\varepsilon^{-1},\log(\delta^{-1}))
, which is attainable by the
Golf
algorithm via the results of
[
XFBJK23
]
(see also
Lemma

E.3
). We obtain this by noting that a realizable model class can be used to construct a realizable value function class
\mathcal{F}
and a complete value function class
\mathcal{G}
of sizes
\log\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert=\log\lvert\mathcal{M}\rvert
and
\log\lvert\mathcal{G}\rvert=\mathcal{O}(\log\lvert\mathcal{M}\rvert)
.

•

Statistical intractability (
✗
): The latent models used in the construction of
Theorem

3.1
are a set of coverable MDPs – in particular, these are trivially coverable with
C_{\texttt{cov}}=1
since there is a single latent model and we can take
\mu=d^{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}},\pi_{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}}}
. We remark that it is an interesting open question whether this impossibility result continues to hold if we require coverability with respect to the class
\Pi
of all possible latent policies.

Known Stochastic MDP (
\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert=1
) (
✗
).

•

Latent class
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
:
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}=\{M_{\texttt{lat}}=(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},P_{\texttt{lat}},R_{\texttt{lat}},H)\}
is a set of MDPs of size 1.

•

Latent complexity
comp
: We take
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=0
, which is attainable as
M_{\texttt{lat}}
is known and we can simply deploy its optimal policy.

•

Statistical intractability (
✗
): This is precisely the setting of
Theorem

3.1
, which shows that at least
\Omega(N/\log(N))
samples will be needed, where
N=\lvert\Phi\rvert
.

Bellman rank (
Q
-type or
V
-type) (
✗
)

•

Latent assumption:
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}=\left\{M_{\texttt{lat}}=(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},P_{\texttt{lat}},R_{\texttt{lat}},H)\right\}
is a set of latent models such that each
M_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
has
Q
-type Bellman rank
d
or
V
-type Bellman rank
d

[
JLM21
]
. Letting
\mathcal{F}
be a realizable value function class for
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
, in the
Q
-type case, this means that the
\lvert\Pi_{\mathcal{F}}\rvert\times\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert
matrix

\mathcal{E}^{Q}_{h}(\pi,f)=\En^{\pi}\left[f_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})-r_{h}-\max_{a^{\prime}}f_{h+1}(s_{h+1},a^{\prime})\right],

admits a rank
d
factorization. In the
V
-type case, the matrix

\mathcal{E}^{V}_{h}(\pi,f)=\En_{s_{h}\sim d^{\pi}_{h},a_{h}\sim\pi_{f}}\left[f_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})-r_{h}-\max_{a^{\prime}}f_{h+1}(s_{h+1},a^{\prime})\right]

admits a rank-
d
matrix factorization.

•

Latent complexity
comp
: We take
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=\poly(d,H,\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert\log\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert,\varepsilon^{-1},\log(\delta^{-1}))
for the
V
-type Bellman rank case, which is achievable by the
Olive
algorithm of
[
JKALS17
]
, and
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=\poly(d,H,\log\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert,\varepsilon^{-1},\log(\delta^{-1}))
for
Q
-type Bellman rank, which is achievable by the
Bilin-Ucb
algorithm of
[
Du+21
]
.

•

Statistical intractability (
✗
): We note that the construction in
Theorem

3.1
has
\left\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\right\rvert=1
, which trivially has Bellman rank equal to
1
, so
Theorem

3.1
precludes statistical modularity with complexity
comp
.

Eluder dimension + Bellman Completeness (
✗
)

•

Latent class
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
:
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}=\left\{M_{\texttt{lat}}=(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},P_{\texttt{lat}},R_{\texttt{lat}},H)\right\}
is a set of MDPs such that there is a function class
\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{lat}}
satisfying

\forall f_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{lat}},M_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}:\quad\mathcal{T}^{M_{\texttt{lat}}}f_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{lat}}.

Furthermore, each
M_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
has Bellman-Eluder dimension bounded by
d
(see Definition 8 of
[
JLM21
]
).

•

Latent complexity
comp
: We take
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=\poly(d,H,\log\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert,\varepsilon^{-1},\log(\delta^{-1}))
, which is attainable by the
Golf
algorithm of
[
JLM21
]
.

•

Statistical intractability (
✗
): As in the Bellman rank case, the construction in
Theorem

3.1
has
\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert=1
, so we can take
\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{lat}}=\{Q^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\star}\mid M_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\}
which is evidently complete for
\mathcal{T}^{M_{\texttt{lat}}}
, and has Eluder dimension
1
, so
Theorem

3.1
precludes statistical modularity with complexity
comp
.

Q^{\star}
-irrelevant State Abstraction (
✗
)

•

Latent class
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
:
M_{\texttt{lat}}=(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},P_{\texttt{lat}},R_{\texttt{lat}},H)
such that there is a known state abstraction function
\zeta_{\texttt{lat}}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}
such that
\zeta_{\texttt{lat}}(s)=\zeta_{\texttt{lat}}(s^{\prime})
implies that
Q^{{\scriptscriptstyle M_{\texttt{lat}}},\star}(s,a)=Q^{{\scriptscriptstyle M_{\texttt{lat}}},\star}(s^{\prime},a)
for all
a\in\mathcal{A}
.

•

Latent complexity
comp
: We take
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=\poly(|\mathcal{Z}|,|\mathcal{A}|,H,\varepsilon^{-1},\log(\delta^{-1}))
which is attainable by the
Olive
algorithm of
[
JKALS17
]
.

•

Statistical intractability (
✗
): We take
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}=\left\{M_{\texttt{lat}}\right\}
as the MDP class from the construction of
Theorem

3.1
. Let
Q^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}\coloneqq Q^{{\scriptscriptstyle M_{\texttt{lat}}},\star}
. Note that we have
Q^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}(s,a)\in\{0,1\}
for all
s,a
, so we can take a latent abstract state space
\mathcal{Z}=\{(0,0),(0,1),(1,0),(1,1)\}
and a state abstraction function
\zeta_{\texttt{lat}}
such that
\zeta_{\texttt{lat}}(s)=(i,j)
if
Q^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}(s,0)=i
and
Q^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}(s,1)=j
. This satisfies the property of a
Q^{\star}
-irrelevant abstraction, since
\zeta_{\texttt{lat}}(s)=\zeta_{\texttt{lat}}(s^{\prime})=(i,j)
implies that
Q^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}(s,0)=Q^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}(s^{\prime},0)=i
and
Q^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}(s,1)=Q^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}(s^{\prime},1)=j
. This has a constant-sized abstract space (
|\mathcal{Z}|=4
) and
|\mathcal{A}|=2
, so
Theorem

3.1
precludes statistical modularity with complexity
comp
.

Linear Mixture MDP (
✗
).

•

Latent class
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
: MDPs
M_{\texttt{lat}}=(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},P_{\texttt{lat}},R_{\texttt{lat}},H)
such that there is a known feature map
\zeta_{\texttt{lat}}=\{\zeta_{\texttt{lat},h}:s^{\prime},s,a\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{d}\}_{h=1}^{H}
such that

\forall h\in[H],\exists\theta_{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\quad P_{\texttt{lat},h}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a)=\left\langle\zeta_{\texttt{lat},h}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a),\theta_{h}\right\rangle

•

Latent complexity
comp
: We take
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=\poly(d,H,\varepsilon^{-1},\log(\delta^{-1}))
, which is attainable by the
Ucrl-Vtr
+
algorithm of
[
ZGS21
]

•

Statistical intractability (
✗
): We take
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}=\{M_{\texttt{lat}}\}
to be the construction of
Theorem

3.1
. Here, there is a single latent model, so this is trivially embeddable with
\zeta_{\texttt{lat},h}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a)=P^{\star}_{\texttt{lat},h}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a)\in\mathbb{R}^{1}
. This has dimension
d=1
, so
Theorem

3.1
precludes statistical modularity with complexity
comp
.

Linear
Q^{\star}/V^{\star}
(
✗
).

•

Latent class
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
: MDPs
M_{\texttt{lat}}=(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},P_{\texttt{lat}},R_{\texttt{lat}},H)
such that there are known features maps
\alpha_{\texttt{lat}}:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\beta_{\texttt{lat}}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that for all
M_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
, there exists unknown parameters
\theta_{Q},\theta_{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
Q^{{\scriptscriptstyle M_{\texttt{lat}}},\star}(s,a)=\left\langle\alpha_{\texttt{lat}}(s,a),\theta_{Q}\right\rangle
and
V^{{\scriptscriptstyle M_{\texttt{lat}}},\star}(s)=\left\langle\beta_{\texttt{lat}}(s),\theta_{V}\right\rangle
.

•

Latent complexity
comp
: We take
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=\poly(d,H,\varepsilon^{-1},\log(\delta^{-1}))
, which is attainable by the
Bilin-Ucb
algorithm of
[
Du+21
]
.

•

Statistical intractability (
✗
): We can take
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
to be the latent MDP class from the construction of
Theorem

3.1
. Since there is a single latent model, this is trivially embeddable with dimension
1
, i.e. we can take
\zeta_{\texttt{lat}}(s,a)=Q^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}(s,a)
and
\beta_{\texttt{lat}}(s)=V^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}(s)
. This has dimension
d=1
, so

3.1
precludes statistical modularity with complexity
comp
.

Low State or State-Action Occupancy (
\forall\,\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle M}:M\in\mathcal{M})
(
✗
).

•

Latent class
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
: In the Low State Occupancy model,
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}=\{M_{\texttt{lat}}=(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},P_{\texttt{lat}},R_{\texttt{lat}},H)\}
is a set of MDPs such that there exists a feature map
\zeta^{V}_{\texttt{lat}}=\left\{\zeta_{\texttt{lat},h}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}\right\}_{h=1}^{H}
such that for all
\pi\in\{\pi_{M_{\texttt{lat}}}\mid M_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\}
and for all
M_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
, we have

\forall h\in[H]\,\,\exists\theta^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\pi}_{h}:\quad d^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\pi}_{h}(s)=\left\langle\zeta^{V}_{\texttt{lat},h}(s),\theta^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\pi}_{h}\right\rangle.

For the State-Action Occupancy model, we have that there exists a feature map
\zeta^{Q}_{\texttt{lat}}=\left\{\zeta_{\texttt{lat},h}:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}\right\}_{h=1}^{H}
such that for all
\pi\in\{\pi_{M_{\texttt{lat}}}\mid M_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\}
and for all
M_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
, we have

\forall h\in[H]\,\,\exists\theta^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\pi}_{h}:\quad d^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\pi}_{h}(s,a)=\left\langle\zeta^{Q}_{\texttt{lat},h}(s,a),\theta^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\pi}_{h}\right\rangle.

Note that the feature map does not need to be known in either case.

•

Latent complexity
comp
: We take
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=\poly(d,\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert,H,\log\lvert\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert,\varepsilon^{-1},\log(\delta^{-1}))
for the state occupancy case and
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=\poly(d,H,\log\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert,\varepsilon^{-1},\log(\delta^{-1}))
. Both are attainable by the
Bilin-Ucb
algorithm of
[
Du+21
]
, since i) MDPs with this property have Bilinear rank bounded by
d\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert
and
d
respectively (see Definition 4.3 and Lemma 4.6 of
[
Du+21
]
), and ii) one can construct the value function class
\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{lat}}=\{Q^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\star}\mid M_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\}
which is realizable and has size
\log\lvert\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert=\log\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert
.

•

Intractability: We can take the construction of
Theorem

3.1
, which has
\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert=1
and thus is trivially embeddable with dimension
1
, i.e. we can take
\zeta^{V}_{\texttt{lat}}(s)=d^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\pi_{M_{\texttt{lat}}}}(s)
and
\zeta^{Q}_{\texttt{lat}}(s,a)=d^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\pi_{M_{\texttt{lat}}}}(s,a)
.

Bisimulation (
?
)

•

Latent class
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
: MDPs
M_{\texttt{lat}}=(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},P_{\texttt{lat}},R_{\texttt{lat}},H)
such that there is a known state abstraction function
\zeta_{\texttt{lat}}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}
such that
\zeta_{\texttt{lat}}(s)=\zeta_{\texttt{lat}}(\widetilde{s})
implies that
R_{\texttt{lat}}(s,a)=R_{\texttt{lat}}(\widetilde{s},a)
for all
a\in\mathcal{A}
as well as
\sum_{s^{\prime}:\zeta_{\texttt{lat}}(s^{\prime})=z^{\prime}}P_{\texttt{lat}}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a)=\sum_{s^{\prime}:\zeta_{\texttt{lat}}(s^{\prime})=z^{\prime}}P_{\texttt{lat}}(s^{\prime}\mid\widetilde{s},a)
for all
z^{\prime}
.

•

Latent complexity
comp
: We take
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=\poly(|\mathcal{Z}|,|\mathcal{A}|,H,\varepsilon^{-1},\log(\delta^{-1}))
which is attainable by the
Olive
algorithm of
[
JKALS17
]
.

•

Openness (
?
): A negative result does not follow from existing constructions, since the dynamics from the tree-based construction of
Theorem

3.1
are not bisimilar unless
\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert=\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert
, which allows for the application of tabular methods. At the same time, a positive result does not follow from existing methods, since it is non-trivial to extend existing Block MDP methods to use the bisimulation state abstraction in a way that only pays for
\lvert\mathcal{Z}\rvert
.

Low State-Action Occupancy (
\forall\pi:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{A})
) (
?
⋆
)

•

Latent class
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
:
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}=\{M_{\texttt{lat}}=(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},P_{\texttt{lat}},R_{\texttt{lat}},H)\}
is a set of MDPs such that there exists a feature map
\zeta^{Q}_{\texttt{lat}}=\left\{\zeta_{\texttt{lat},h}:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}\right\}_{h=1}^{H}
such that for all
\pi:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{A})
and for all
M_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
, we have

\forall h\in[H]\,\,\exists\theta^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\pi}_{h}:\quad d^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\pi}_{h}(s,a)=\left\langle\zeta^{Q}_{\texttt{lat},h}(s,a),\theta^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\pi}_{h}\right\rangle.

Note that the feature map does not need to be known.

•

We take
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=\poly(d,H,\log\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert,\varepsilon^{-1},\log(\delta^{-1}))
, which is attainable by the
Bilin-Ucb
algorithm of
[
Du+21
]
, since i) MDPs with this property have Bilinear rank bounded by
d
(see Definition 4.3 and Lemma 4.6 of
[
