]
), and ii) one can construct a realizable value function class of size
\log\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert=\log\lvert\mathcal{M}\rvert
.

•

Openness (
?
): A negative result does not follow from existing constructions, since the dynamics from the tree-based construction of
Theorem

3.1
do not have linear occupancies for all
\pi:\mathcal{S}\to\Delta(\mathcal{A})
unless
d=\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert
, which allows for the application of tabular methods, and the dynamics from the bandit-based construction
Theorem

D.1
do not have linear occupancies for all
\pi:\mathcal{S}\to\Delta(\mathcal{A})
unless
d=\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert
. At the same time, unlike the low state occupancy case, a positive result does not follow as it is unclear if we can express the observation-space occupancies linearly.

•

Statistical tractability with additional (suboptimal)
\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert
-dependence (
✓
): Note that we can reduce to the Low State Occupancy case (
✓
), since

d^{\pi}(s)=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}d^{\pi}(s,a)=\left\langle\theta^{\pi},\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\zeta^{Q}_{\texttt{lat}}(s,a)\right\rangle\coloneqq\left\langle\theta^{\pi},\zeta^{V}_{\texttt{lat}}(s)\right\rangle.

However, this blows up the feature norm bound of the feature map
\zeta^{V}_{\texttt{lat}}(s)
by a factor of
\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert
, which will appear logarithmically in the bound obtained by
Bilin-Ucb
.

Model class + Coverability (
\forall\,\pi:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{A})
) (
?
).

•

Latent class
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
:
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}=\left\{M_{\texttt{lat}}=(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},P_{\texttt{lat}},R_{\texttt{lat}},H)\right\}
is a set of MDPs that all satisfy coverability with respect to all policies
{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{A})
, i.e. we have

\forall M_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}:\quad C_{\texttt{cov}}(M_{\texttt{lat}})=\inf_{\mu_{h}\in\Delta(\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A})}\sup_{h\in[H]}\sup_{\pi:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{A})}\left\|\frac{d^{{\scriptscriptstyle M_{\texttt{lat}}},\pi}_{h}}{\mu_{h}}\right\|_{\infty}<\infty

•

Latent complexity
comp
: We take
\texttt{comp}(\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\varepsilon,\delta)=\poly(C_{\texttt{cov}},H,\log\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert,\varepsilon^{-1},\log(\delta^{-1}))
, which is attainable by the
Golf
algorithm via the results of
[
XFBJK23
]
(see also
Lemma

E.3
). We obtain this by noting that a realizable model class can be used to construct a realizable value function class
\mathcal{F}
and a complete value function class
\mathcal{G}
of sizes
\log\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert=\log\lvert\mathcal{M}\rvert
and
\log\lvert\mathcal{G}\rvert=\mathcal{O}(\log\lvert\mathcal{M}\rvert)
.

•

Openness (
?
): A negative result does not follow from the existing constructions. The tree-based construction of
Theorem

3.1
satisfies coverability with
C_{\texttt{cov}}=\exp(\Omega(H))
and the bandit-based construction of
Theorem

D.1
satisfies coverability with
C_{\texttt{cov}}=\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert
. In both cases, the lower bounds cannot be used to rule out statistical modularity with the above latent complexity. Similarly, it unclear how to obtain a positive result for the latent-dynamics class
\llangle M_{\texttt{lat}},\Phi\rrangle
.

D.3
Proofs for Lower Bounds (
Theorems

3.1
and
D.1
)

D.3.1
Main lower bound (
Theorem

3.1
)

We will prove the following result.

See
3.1

Proof.
Let
N
be given and assume without loss
of generality that it is a power of
2
. We first construct the
class of latent-dynamics MDPs, following
[
SWFK24
]
.

Latent MDP.

The construction has a single “known”
latent MDP
M_{\texttt{lat}}
, so that the only uncertainty in the family of latent-dynamics MDPs we
construct arises from the emission processes. We set
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}=\left\{M_{\texttt{lat}}\right\}
. Set

H=\log_{2}(N)+1
and
\mathcal{A}=\{0,1\}
. We define the state space
and latent transition dynamics as follows.

•

The state space can be partitioned as

\mathcal{S}=\mathcal{S}^{1},\ldots,\mathcal{S}^{N}
.

•

Each block
\mathcal{S}^{i}
corresponds to a standard
depth-
H
binary
tree MDP with deterministic dynamics (e.g.,

[
OVR16
,
DMKV21
]
). There is a
single “root” node at layer
h=1
, which we denote by

s_{\mathsf{root}}^{i}
, and
N
“leaf” nodes at layer
H
, which
we denote by
\big\{s_{\mathsf{leaf}}^{i,j}\big\}_{j\in[N]}
. For
each
h=1,\ldots,H-1
, choosing action
0
leads to the left
successor of the current state deterministically, and
choosing action
1
leads to the right sucessor; this
process continues until we reach a leaf node at layer
H
.

•

The initial state distribution is

P_{\texttt{lat},1}(\emptyset)=\texttt{Unif}(s_{\mathsf{root}}^{1},\ldots,s_{\mathsf{root}}^{N})
.

•

There are no rewards for layers
1,\ldots,H-1
. For
layer
H
, the reward is

\displaystyle R_{H}(s_{\mathsf{leaf}}^{i,j},\cdot)=\mathbb{I}\left\{j=i\right\}.

(18)

This construction can summarized as follows. At layer
1
,
we draw the index of one of
N
binary trees uniformly at random, and
initialize into the root of the tree. From here, we receive a reward
of
1
if we successfully navigate to the leaf node whose index agrees with the
index of the tree itself, and receive a reward of
0
otherwise.

Note that the total number of latent states in this construction is

\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert=N\cdot\lvert\mathcal{S}_{1}\rvert=N(2N-1)

Observation space and decoder class.

Let us introduce some additional notation. For each block

\mathcal{S}^{i}
, let

\mathcal{S}_{h}^{i}\vcentcolon={}\{s_{h}^{i,j}\}_{j\in[2^{h-1}]}
denote the
states in block
i
that are reachable at layer
h
, so that

\mathcal{S}^{i}_{1}=\left\{s_{\mathsf{root}}^{i}\right\}
and

\mathcal{S}^{i}_{H}=\{s_{\mathsf{leaf}}^{i,j}\}_{j\in[N]}
. We define

\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{S}
so that
\lvert\mathcal{X}\rvert\leq{}4N^{2}
, and consider a class of emission processes corresponding to
deterministic maps. Let
\Sigma
denote the set of cyclic permutations on
N
elements, excluding the identity permutation. That is, each
\sigma_{i}\in\Sigma
takes the form

\sigma_{i}:k\mapsto{}k+i\mod{}N\quad\text{ for }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}.

For each

\sigma\in\Sigma
, we consider the emission process

\psi^{\sigma}_{h}(\cdot\mid{}s_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i,j)}})=\mathbb{I}_{s_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(\sigma(i),j)}}}.

That is,
\psi^{\sigma}
shifts the index of the binary tree containing

s_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i,j)}}
according to
\sigma
. Let
\Psi=\{\psi^{\sigma}\mid\sigma\in\Sigma\}
. Consider the decoder class

\displaystyle\Phi=\Psi^{-1}\coloneqq\big\{s^{i}\mapsto{}s^{\psi^{-1}(i)}\mid{}\psi\in\Psi\big\},

which has
\lvert\Phi\rvert=N
. We consider the class of rich-observation MDPs given by

\displaystyle\left\llangle\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\Phi\right\rrangle\vcentcolon={}\left\{M^{i}\vcentcolon={}\llangle M_{\texttt{lat}},\psi^{\sigma_{i}}\rrangle\mid{}\sigma_{i}\in\Sigma\right\}.

(19)

It is clear that this class of rich-observation MDPs satisfies the
decodability assumption for emissions
\Psi
.

Sample complexity lower bound.

To lower bound the sample
complexity, we prove a lower bound on the constrained PAC
Decision-Estimation Coefficient (DEC) of

[
FGH23
]
. For an arbitrary MDP
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}
(defined over
the space
\mathcal{X}
) and
\varepsilon\in[0,2^{1/2}]
, define
16
16

16

For
measures
\mathbb{P}
and
\mathbb{Q}
, we define squared Hellinger distance by
D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q}\right)=\int(\sqrt{d\mathbb{P}}-\sqrt{d\mathbb{Q}})^{2}
.

\displaystyle\mathsf{dec}_{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M})=\inf_{p,q\in\Delta(\Pi)}\sup_{M\in\mathcal{M}}\left\{\En_{\pi\sim{}p}\left[J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M}}(\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle M}})-J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M}}(\pi)\right]\mid{}\En_{\pi\sim{}q}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M(\pi),\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}(\pi)\right)\right]\leq\varepsilon^{2}\right\},

where
M(\pi)
denotes the law over trajectories

(x_{1},a_{1},r_{1}),\ldots,(x_{H},a_{H},r_{H})
induced by executing
the policy
\pi
in the MDP
M
,
J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M}}(\pi)
denotes the expected
reward for policy
\pi
under
M
, and
\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle M}}
denotes the optimal
policy for
M
. We further define

\displaystyle\mathsf{dec}_{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})=\sup_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}}\mathsf{dec}_{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}),

where the supremum ranges over all MDPs defined over
\mathcal{X}
and

\mathcal{A}
. We now appeal to the following technical lemma.

Lemma D.1
.

For all
\varepsilon^{2}\geq{}4/N
, we have that

\mathsf{dec}_{\varepsilon}(\left\llangle\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\Phi\right\rrangle)\geq\frac{1}{2}
.

In light of
Lemma

D.1
, it follows from Theorem 2.1 in
[
FGH23
]
17
17

17

Theorem 2.1 in

[
FGH23
]
is stated with respect to

\sup_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}\in\texttt{conv}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{dec}_{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M})
, but the actual proof (Section
2.2) gives a stronger result that scales with
\sup_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}}\mathsf{dec}_{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M})
.

that any PAC RL algorithm that uses
T
episodes
of interaction for
T\log(T)\leq{}c\cdot{}N
must have

\En\left[J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M}}(\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle M}})-J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M}}(\widehat{\pi})\right]\geq{}c^{\prime}
for a worst-case MDP in

\mathcal{M}
, where
c,c^{\prime}>0
are absolute constants. This implies that any PAC RL which has
\En\left[J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M}}(\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle M}})-J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M}}(\widehat{\pi})\right]\leq{}c^{\prime}
must have
T\log(T)\geq{}c\cdot{}N
and thus
T\geq{}c\cdot N/\log(N)
.

∎

Proof of
Lemma

D.1
.

Define
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
as the latent-space MDP that has identical
dynamics to
M_{\texttt{lat}}
but, has zero reward in every state, and define

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}\vcentcolon=\left\llangle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\texttt{id}\right\rrangle
as the rich-observation MDP obtained
by composing
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
with the “identity” emission process
id
that
sets
x_{h}=s_{h}
. Observe that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}
and
M^{i}
, induce identical dynamics
in observation space if rewards are ignored: For all policies
\pi
,

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:identical_{d}ynamics}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[(x_{1},a_{1}),\ldots,(x_{H},a_{H})=\cdot\right]=\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}},\pi}\left[(x_{1},a_{1}),\ldots,(x_{H},a_{H})=\cdot\right].

(20)

It follows that for each
i
, for all policies
\pi
, we have

\displaystyle D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M^{i}(\pi),\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}(\pi)\right)

\displaystyle=D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left((\left\llangle M_{\texttt{lat}},\psi_{i}\right\rrangle)(\pi),(\left\llangle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\texttt{id}\right\rrangle)(\pi)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[x_{H}=s_{\mathsf{leaf}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(\psi_{i}(j),j)}}\right]\cdot D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(\mathbb{I}_{1},\mathbb{I}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=2\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[x_{H}=s_{\mathsf{leaf}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(\psi_{i}(j),j)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[x_{H}=s_{\mathsf{leaf}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(\psi_{i}(j),j)}}\mid{}x_{1}=s_{\mathsf{root}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(\psi_{i}(j))}}\right],

(21)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[x_{H}=s_{\mathsf{leaf}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(j,\psi_{i}^{-1}(j))}}\mid{}x_{1}=s_{\mathsf{root}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(j)}}\right],

(22)

since the learner receives identical feedback in the MDPs

M^{i}
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}
unless they reach the observation

x_{H}=s_{\mathsf{leaf}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(\psi_{i}(j),j)}}
for some
j
(corresponding to latent
state
s_{\mathsf{leaf}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(j,j)}}
in
M^{i}
), in which case they receiver
reward
1
in
M^{i}
but reward
0
in
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}
.
We now claim that for any
q\in\Delta(\Pi)
, there exists a set of
at least
N/2
indices
\mathcal{I}_{q}\subset[N]
such that

\displaystyle\En_{\pi\sim{}q}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M^{i}(\pi),\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}(\pi)\right)\right]\leq\frac{4}{N}

(23)

for all
i\in\mathcal{I}_{q}
. To see this, note that by

Eq. (
22
)
, we have

\displaystyle\En_{i\sim\texttt{Unif}([N])}\En_{\pi\sim{}q}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M^{i}(\pi),\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}(\pi)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq{}\En_{\pi\sim{}q}\left[\frac{2}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[x_{H}=s_{\mathsf{leaf}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(j,\psi_{i}^{-1}(j))}}\mid{}x_{1}=s_{\mathsf{root}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(j)}}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq{}\En_{\pi\sim{}q}\left[\frac{2}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{1}{N}\right]=\frac{2}{N},

where the second inequality uses that

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[x_{H}=s_{\mathsf{leaf}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(j,\psi_{i}^{-1}(j))}}\mid{}x_{1}=s_{\mathsf{root}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(j)}}\right]\leq{}1
, as the events in the sum are mutually exclusive (and the event we
condition on does not depend on
i
). We conclude by Markov’s
inequality that

\mathbb{P}_{i\sim\texttt{Unif}([N])}\left[\En_{\pi\sim{}q}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M^{i}(\pi),\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}(\pi)\right)\right]\geq{}4/N\right]\leq{}1/2
,
giving
\mathcal{I}_{q}\geq{}N/2
.

From
Eq. (
27
)
, we conclude that for all

\varepsilon^{2}\geq{}4/N
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{dec}_{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M})\geq{}\inf_{q\in\Delta(\Pi)}\inf_{p\in\Delta(\Pi)}\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{q}}\left\{\En_{\pi\sim{}p}\left[J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}}(\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}})-J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}}(\pi)\right]\right\}.

To lower bound this quantity, observe that for any index
i
and any
policy
\pi
, we have

\displaystyle J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}}(\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}})-J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}}(\pi)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}},\pi}\left[x_{H}\neq s_{\mathsf{leaf}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(\psi_{i}(j),j)}}\mid{}x_{1}=s_{\mathsf{root}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(\psi_{i}(j))}}\right]

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}},\pi}\left[x_{H}=s_{\mathsf{leaf}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(\psi_{i}(j),j)}}\mid{}x_{1}=s_{\mathsf{root}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(\psi_{i}(j))}}\right]

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[x_{H}=s_{\mathsf{leaf}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(\psi_{i}(j),j)}}\mid{}x_{1}=s_{\mathsf{root}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(\psi_{i}(j))}}\right]

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[x_{H}=s_{\mathsf{leaf}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(j,\psi_{i}^{-1}(j))}}\mid{}x_{1}=s_{\mathsf{root}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(j)}}\right],

where the third inequality uses
Eq. (
20
)
.
We conclude that for any distribution
p,q\in\Delta(\Pi)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{q}}\left\{\En_{\pi\sim{}p}\left[J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}}(\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}})-J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}}(\pi)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\En_{i\sim\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{I}_{q})}\left\{\En_{\pi\sim{}p}\left[J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}}(\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}})-J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}}(\pi)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\En_{i\sim\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{I}_{q})}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[x_{H}=s_{\mathsf{leaf}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(j,\psi_{i}^{-1}(j))}}\mid{}x_{1}=s_{\mathsf{root}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(j)}}\right]

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\mathcal{I}_{q}\right\rvert}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{q}}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[x_{H}=s_{\mathsf{leaf}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(j,\psi_{i}^{-1}(j))}}\mid{}x_{1}=s_{\mathsf{root}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(j)}}\right]\geq{}1-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\mathcal{I}_{q}\right\rvert}\geq{}\frac{1}{2}

as long as
N\geq{}4
, where the second-to-last inequality uses that
for all
j
, the events

\big\{x_{H}=s_{\mathsf{leaf}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(j,\psi_{i}^{-1}(j))}}\mid{}x_{1}=s_{\mathsf{root}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(j)}}\big\}

are disjoint for all
i
. Since this lower bound holds uniformly for
all
q,p\in\Delta(\Pi)
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathsf{dec}_{\varepsilon}(\left\llangle\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\Phi\right\rrangle,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M})\geq\frac{1}{2}.

∎

D.3.2
Proof of alternative lower bound (
Theorem

D.1
)

We will prove the following result.

See
D.1

Proof of
Theorem

D.1
.

We repeat more or less repeat the same proof as

Theorem

3.1
, but with the appropriate modifications
to translate from the contextual tree-based construction in

Theorem

3.1
to the contextual bandit-based
construction in the theorem statement. Let
N
be given and assume without loss of generality that it is a power of
2
.

Latent MDP.

Our construction has a single “known” latent MDP
M_{\texttt{lat}}
; that is,
the only uncertainty in the family of rich-observation MDPs we
construct arises from the emission processes. Set
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}=\left\{M_{\texttt{lat}}\right\}
. Set

H=1
and
\mathcal{A}=[N]
. We define the state space
and latent transition dynamics as follows.

•

The state space can be partitioned as

\mathcal{S}=\mathcal{S}^{1},\ldots,\mathcal{S}^{N}
.

•

Each block
\mathcal{S}^{i}
corresponds to a single state
s^{i}
with
N
actions denoted by
a^{i}
,
i\in[N]
.

•

The initial state distribution is

P_{\texttt{lat},1}(\emptyset)=\texttt{Unif}(s^{1},\ldots,s^{N})
.

•

The reward function is

\displaystyle R_{1}(s^{i},a^{j})=\mathbb{I}\left\{j=i\right\}.

(24)

Informally, this construction can summarized as a contextual bandit (with uniform context distribution), with a reward of
1
if and only if we play the action corresponding to the index of the context drawn.

Note that the total number of latent states in this construction is

\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert=N
and the number of actions is
\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert=N
.

Observation space and decoder class.

We define

\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{S}
so that
\lvert\mathcal{X}\rvert=\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert
, and consider a class of emission processes corresponding to
deterministic maps. Let
\Sigma
denote the set of cyclic permutations on
N
elements, excluding the identity permutation. That is, each
\sigma_{i}\in\Sigma
takes the form

\sigma_{i}:k\mapsto{}k+i\mod{}N,\quad\text{ for }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}.

For each

\sigma\in\Sigma
, we consider the emission process

\psi^{\sigma}(\cdot\mid{}s^{i})=\mathbb{I}_{s^{\sigma(i)}}(\cdot)

That is,
\psi^{\sigma}
shifts the context
s^{i}
according to
\sigma
. Let
\Psi=\{\psi^{\sigma}\mid\sigma\in\Sigma\}
. Consider the decoder class

\displaystyle\Phi=\Psi^{-1}\coloneqq\big\{s^{i}\mapsto{}s^{\psi^{-1}(i)}\mid{}\psi\in\Psi\big\},

which has
\lvert\Phi\rvert=N
. We consider the class of rich-observation MDPs given by

\displaystyle\left\llangle\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\Phi\right\rrangle\vcentcolon={}\left\{M^{i}\vcentcolon={}\llangle M_{\texttt{lat}},\psi^{\sigma_{i}}\rrangle\mid{}\sigma_{i}\in\Sigma\right\}.

(25)

It is clear that this class of rich-observation MDPs satisfies the
decodability assumption for emissions
\Psi
.

Sample complexity lower bound.

To lower bound the sample
complexity, we prove a lower bound on the constrained PAC
Decision-Estimation Coefficient (DEC) of

[
FGH23
]
. For an arbitrary MDP
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}
(defined over
the space
\mathcal{X}
) and
\varepsilon\in[0,2^{1/2}]
, define
18
18

18

For
measures
\mathbb{P}
and
\mathbb{Q}
, we define squared Hellinger distance by
D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q}\right)=\int(\sqrt{d\mathbb{P}}-\sqrt{d\mathbb{Q}})^{2}
.

\displaystyle\mathsf{dec}_{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M})=\inf_{p,q\in\Delta(\Pi)}\sup_{M\in\mathcal{M}}\left\{\En_{\pi\sim{}p}\left[J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M}}(\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle M}})-J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M}}(\pi)\right]\mid{}\En_{\pi\sim{}q}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M(\pi),\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}(\pi)\right)\right]\leq\varepsilon^{2}\right\},

where
M(\pi)
denotes the law over observations

(x_{1},a_{1},r_{1})
induced by executing
the policy
\pi
in the MDP
M
,
J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M}}(\pi)
denotes the expected
reward for policy
\pi
under
M
, and
\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle M}}
denotes the optimal
policy for
M
. We further define

\displaystyle\mathsf{dec}_{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M})=\sup_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}}\mathsf{dec}_{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}),

where the supremum ranges over all MDPs defined over
\mathcal{X}
and

\mathcal{A}
. We now appeal to the following technical lemma.

Lemma D.2
.

For all
\varepsilon^{2}\geq{}4/N
, we have that

\sup_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}}\mathsf{dec}_{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M})\geq\frac{1}{2}
.

In light of
Lemma

D.2
, it follows from Theorem 2.1 in
[
FGH23
]
19
19

19

Theorem 2.1 in

[
FGH23
]
is stated with respect to

\sup_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}\in\texttt{conv}(\mathcal{M})}\mathsf{dec}_{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M})
, but the actual proof (Section
2.2) gives a stronger result that scales with
\sup_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}}\mathsf{dec}_{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{M},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M})
.

that any PAC RL algorithm that uses
T
episodes
of interaction for
T\log(T)\leq{}c\cdot{}N
must have

\En\left[J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M}}(\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle M}})-J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M}}(\widehat{\pi})\right]\geq{}c^{\prime}
for a worst-case MDP in

\mathcal{M}
, where
c,c^{\prime}>0
are absolute constants. This implies that any PAC RL which has
\En\left[J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M}}(\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle M}})-J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M}}(\widehat{\pi})\right]\leq{}c^{\prime}
must have
T\log(T)\geq{}c\cdot{}N
and thus
T\geq{}c\cdot N/\log(N)
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

D.2
.

Define
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
as the latent-space MDP that has identical
dynamics to
M_{\texttt{lat}}
but, has zero reward for every state-action pair, and define

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}\vcentcolon=\left\llangle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\texttt{id}\right\rrangle
as the rich-observation MDP obtained
by composing
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
with the identity emission process that
sets
x_{h}=s_{h}
. In the rest of the proof, we use the shorthand
\psi_{i}\coloneqq\psi^{\sigma_{i}}
. Observe that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}
and
M^{i}
, induce identical dynamics
in observation space if rewards are ignored, i.e. for all policies
\pi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{A})
,

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:identical_{d}ynamics_{c}b}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[(x_{1},a_{1})=\cdot\right]=\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}},\pi}\left[(x_{1},a_{1})=\cdot\right].

(26)

It follows that for each
i
, for all policies
\pi
, we have

\displaystyle D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M^{i}(\pi),\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}(\pi)\right)

\displaystyle=D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left((\left\llangle M_{\texttt{lat}},\psi_{i}\right\rrangle)(\pi),(\left\llangle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\texttt{id}\right\rrangle)(\pi)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[x_{1}=s^{\psi_{i}(j)},a_{1}=a^{j}\right]\cdot D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(\mathbb{I}_{1},\mathbb{I}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=2\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[x_{1}=s^{\psi_{i}(j)},a_{1}=a^{j}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[a_{1}=a^{j}\mid x_{1}=s^{\psi_{i}(j)}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[a_{1}=a^{\psi^{-1}_{i}(j)}\mid x_{1}=s^{j}\right]

since the learner receives identical feedback in the MDPs

M^{i}
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}
unless they play the action

a_{1}=a^{j}
given observation
x_{1}=s^{\psi_{i}(j)}
(corresponding to latent
state
s^{i}
in
M^{i}
), in which case they receiver
reward
1
in
M^{i}
but reward
0
in
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}
.
We now claim that for any
q\in\Delta(\Pi)
, there exists a set of
at least
N/2
indices
\mathcal{I}_{q}\subset[N]
such that

\displaystyle\En_{\pi\sim{}q}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M^{i}(\pi),\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}(\pi)\right)\right]\leq\frac{4}{N}

(27)

for all
i\in\mathcal{I}_{q}
. To see this, note that by

Eq. (
22
)
, we have

\displaystyle\En_{i\sim\texttt{Unif}([N])}\En_{\pi\sim{}q}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M^{i}(\pi),\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}(\pi)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq{}\En_{\pi\sim{}q}\left[\frac{2}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[a_{1}=a^{\psi^{-1}_{i}(j)}\mid x_{1}=j\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq{}\En_{\pi\sim{}q}\left[\frac{2}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{1}{N}\right]=\frac{2}{N}.

We conclude by Markov’s
inequality that

\mathbb{P}_{i\sim\texttt{Unif}([N])}\left[\En_{\pi\sim{}q}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M^{i}(\pi),\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}(\pi)\right)\right]\geq{}4/N\right]\leq{}1/2
,
giving
\mathcal{I}_{q}\geq{}N/2
.

From
Eq. (
27
)
, we conclude that for all

\varepsilon^{2}\geq{}4/N
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{dec}_{\varepsilon}(\left\llangle\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\Phi\right\rrangle,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M})\geq{}\inf_{q\in\Delta(\Pi)}\inf_{p\in\Delta(\Pi)}\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{q}}\left\{\En_{\pi\sim{}p}\left[J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}}(\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}})-J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}}(\pi)\right]\right\}.

To lower bound this quantity, observe that for any index
i
and any
policy
\pi
, we have

\displaystyle J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}}(\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}})-J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}}(\pi)

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}},\pi}\left[a_{1}=a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(j)}}\mid{}x_{1}=s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(\psi_{i}(j))}}\right]

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[a_{1}=a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(j)}}\mid{}x_{1}=s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(\psi_{i}(j))}}\right]

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[a_{1}=a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(\psi^{-1}_{i}(j))}}\mid{}x_{1}=s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(j)}}\right],

where the third inequality uses
Eq. (
26
)
.
We conclude that for any distribution
p,q\in\Delta(\Pi)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{q}}\left\{\En_{\pi\sim{}p}\left[J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}}(\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}})-J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}}(\pi)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\En_{i\sim\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{I}_{q})}\left\{\En_{\pi\sim{}p}\left[J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}}(\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}})-J^{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{i}}}(\pi)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\En_{i\sim\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{I}_{q})}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[a_{1}=a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(\psi^{-1}_{i}(j))}}\mid{}x_{1}=s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(j)}}\right]

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{1}{\left\lvert\mathcal{I}_{q}\right\rvert}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{q}}\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}},\pi}\left[a_{1}=a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(\psi^{-1}_{i}(j))}}\mid{}x_{1}=s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(j)}}\right]\geq{}1-\frac{1}{\left\lvert\mathcal{I}_{q}\right\rvert}\geq{}\frac{1}{2}

as long as
N\geq{}4
. Since this lower bound holds uniformly for
all
q,p\in\Delta(\Pi)
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathsf{dec}_{\varepsilon}(\left\llangle\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\Phi\right\rrangle,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M})\geq\frac{1}{2}.

∎

Appendix E
Proofs for
Section

3.3
: Positive Results

This section is dedicated to the proof of our upper bound establishing that pushforward-coverable MDPs are statistically modular (
Theorem

3.2
).

E.1
Proofs for Latent Model Class + Pushforward Coverability (
Theorem

3.2
)

In this section, we establish positive results under latent MDP classes which satisfy pushforward coverability. We assume that every model in
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
satisfies
pushforward coverability
, defined as follows:

Definition E.1

(Pushforward coverability)
.

The pushforward coverability coefficient
C_{\texttt{push}}
for an MDP
M
with transition kernel
P
is defined by

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:pushforward-coverability}C_{\texttt{push}}(M)=\max_{h\in[H]}\inf_{\mu\in\Delta(\mathcal{S})}\sup_{(s,a,s^{\prime})\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{S}}\frac{P_{h-1}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a)}{\mu(s^{\prime})}.

(28)

The pushforward coverability coefficient for an MDP class
\mathcal{M}
is defined by

C_{\texttt{push}}(\mathcal{M})=\max_{M\in\mathcal{M}}C_{\texttt{push}}(M).

Note that for any MDP
M
we always have

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:coverability-pushforward-bound}C_{\texttt{cov}}(M,\Pi_{\texttt{rns}})\leq C_{\texttt{push}}(M)\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert,

(29)

where
C_{\texttt{cov}}
is the state-action coverability coefficient (
Definition

C.3
). Thus, an MDP with low pushforward coverability is also an MDP with low state-action coverability for all policies (upto a dependence on
\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert
).

We will show the show the following result.

See
3.2

The proof comes in three parts. We will firstly show that MDP that satisfies pushforward coverability admit low-dimensional feature maps that can approximate Bellman backups (
Section

E.1.1
), then establish that a regret bound for the
Golf
algorithm
[
XFBJK23
]
under misspecification (
Section

E.1.2
), and then combine these ingredients (
Section

E.1.3
).

E.1.1
A structural result: Pushforward-coverable MDPs are
approximately low-rank

Our central technical result for this section is
Lemma

E.1
, which is
based on a variant of the Johnson-Lindenstrauss lemma and establishes that under pushforward coverability, we can define a linear feature class which satisfies
an approximate form of Bellman completeness. We define the clipping operator via

\texttt{clip}_{[0,2]}(x)\coloneqq\max\left\{\min\left\{x,2\right\},0\right\}.

Lemma E.1

(Existence of a low-dimensional embedding)
.

Let
M
be a known MDP with reward function
r
, transition kernel
P
, and pushforward coverability parameter
C_{\texttt{push}}
. Let
\mu=\{\mu_{h}\}_{h\in[H]}
denote its pushforward coverability distribution (i.e. the minimizer of
Definition

3.3
) and
\mathcal{F}\subseteq(\mathcal{S}\times[H]\to[0,1])
be an arbitrary class of functions. Suppose that we
sample
W\in\left\{\pm{}1\right\}^{d\times\mathcal{S}}
as a matrix of independent Rademacher
random variables, and define

\displaystyle\psi_{h}(s,a)=r_{h}(s,a)\oplus\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W\left(P_{h}(\cdot\mid s,a)/\mu^{1/2}_{h}(\cdot)\right)_{\cdot\in\mathcal{S}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d+1}

and

\displaystyle w_{f,h}=1\oplus\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}W\left(\mu^{1/2}_{h}(\cdot)f_{h+1}(\cdot)\right)_{\cdot\in\mathcal{S}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d+1}.

Then for any
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}\in(0,1)
, as long as we set

d\geq 2^{9}\frac{C_{\texttt{push}}\log(16\abs{\cF}H\deltainv/\vepsapx)}{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}},

we have that with probability at least
1-\delta
, for all
f\in\mathcal{F}
and
h\in[H]
,

\displaystyle\En_{\mu_{h}\otimes\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{A})}\left[\left(\texttt{clip}_{[0,2]}\left[\left\langle w_{f,h},\psi_{h}(s,a)\right\rangle-\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h+1}(s,a)\right]\right)^{2}\right]\leq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}},

as well as
\max_{s,a,h}\left\|\psi_{h}(s,a)\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{\texttt{push}}(16\log(\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert H)+11)
and
\max_{f,h}\left\|w_{f,h}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq{}16\log(\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert H)+11
. We emphasize that the feature map
\psi=\left\{\psi_{h}\right\}_{h=1}^{H}
is
oblivious to
\mathcal{F}
, in the sense that it can
be computed directly from
M
without any knowledge of
\mathcal{F}
.

Proof of
Lemma

E.1
.
Fix
h\in[H]
, whose dependence we omit for cleanliness. We begin by verifying that, in expectation,
\langle w_{f},\psi(s,a)\rangle
is equal to
\mathcal{T}f(s,a)
. For this, note that

\displaystyle\left\langle w_{f},\psi(s,a)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=r(s,a)+\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}W_{i,s^{\prime}}\frac{P(s^{\prime}\mid s,a)}{\mu^{1/2}(s^{\prime})}\right)\left(\sum_{s^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}W_{i,s^{\prime\prime}}\mu^{1/2}(s^{\prime\prime})f(s^{\prime\prime})\right)

\displaystyle=r(s,a)+\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}P(s^{\prime}\mid s,a)f(s^{\prime})+\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{S}\\
s^{\prime\prime}\neq s^{\prime}\end{subarray}}W_{i,s^{\prime}}\frac{P(s^{\prime}\mid s,a)}{\mu^{1/2}(s^{\prime})}W_{i,s^{\prime\prime}}\mu^{1/2}(s^{\prime\prime})f(s^{\prime\prime}).

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathcal{T}f(s,a)-\left\langle w_{f},\psi(s,a)\right\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{S}\\
s^{\prime\prime}\neq s^{\prime}\end{subarray}}W_{i,s^{\prime}}\frac{P(s^{\prime}\mid s,a)}{\mu^{1/2}(s^{\prime})}W_{i,s^{\prime\prime}}\mu^{1/2}(s^{\prime\prime})f(s^{\prime\prime})\right\rvert.

(30)

Note that this remaining noise term is zero-mean – we will show in the sequel that it can be made small by picking
d
appropriately. We next examine the norms of the vectors
\psi(s,a)
and
w_{f}
. Note that we have

\displaystyle\left\|\psi(s,a)\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}W_{i,s^{\prime}}\frac{P(s^{\prime}\mid s,a)}{\mu^{1/2}(s^{\prime})}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{P^{2}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a)}{\mu(s^{\prime})}+\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{S}\\
s^{\prime\prime}\neq s^{\prime}\end{subarray}}W_{i,s^{\prime}}W_{i,s^{\prime\prime}}\frac{P(s^{\prime}\mid s,a)}{\mu^{1/2}(s^{\prime})}\frac{P(s^{\prime\prime}\mid s,a)}{\mu^{1/2}(s^{\prime\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\texttt{push}}+\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{S}\\
s^{\prime\prime}\neq s^{\prime}\end{subarray}}W_{i,s^{\prime}}W_{i,s^{\prime\prime}}\frac{P(s^{\prime}\mid s,a)}{\mu^{1/2}(s^{\prime})}\frac{P(s^{\prime\prime}\mid s,a)}{\mu^{1/2}(s^{\prime\prime})},

(31)

where we have used that

\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{P^{2}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a)}{\mu(s^{\prime})}\leq C_{\texttt{push}}\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}P(s^{\prime}\mid s,a)=C_{\texttt{push}}

by definition of pushforward coverability. Further note that we have

\displaystyle\left\|w_{f}\right\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}W_{i,s^{\prime}}\mu^{1/2}(s^{\prime})f(s^{\prime})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\En_{s^{\prime}\sim\mu}[f(s^{\prime})]+\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{S}\\
s^{\prime\prime}\neq s^{\prime}\end{subarray}}W_{i,s^{\prime}}W_{i,s^{\prime\prime}}\mu^{1/2}(s^{\prime})f(s^{\prime})\cdot\mu^{1/2}(s^{\prime\prime})f(s^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\leq 1+\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{S}\\
s^{\prime\prime}\neq s^{\prime}\end{subarray}}W_{i,s^{\prime}}W_{i,s^{\prime\prime}}\mu^{1/2}(s^{\prime})f(s^{\prime})\cdot\mu^{1/2}(s^{\prime\prime})f(s^{\prime\prime}).

(32)

We will now appeal to the following technical lemma to upper bound
Eq. (
30
)
,
Eq. (
31
)
, and
Eq. (
32
)
by establishing that the Rademacher noise terms concentrate to their expectations. The proof of the lemma will be given in the sequel.

Lemma E.2
.

Let
u,v\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and let
W\in\left\{\pm{}1\right\}^{d\times{}n}
have
independent Rademacher entries. Then with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:rademacher}\left\lvert\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{j\in[n]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[n]\\
k\neq{}j\end{subarray}}W_{i,j}W_{i,k}u_{j}v_{k}\right\rvert\leq{}\left\|u\right\|_{2}\left\|v\right\|_{2}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{32\log(2\delta^{-1})}{d}}+\left\|u\right\|^{2}_{2}\left\|v\right\|^{2}_{2}\cdot\frac{64\log(2\delta^{-1})}{d}.

(33)

Furthermore, for any set of vectors
\mathcal{V}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we also have

\frac{1}{d}\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{j\in[n]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[n]\\
k\neq{}j\end{subarray}}W_{i,j}W_{i,k}v_{j}v_{k}\leq{}\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\|v\|_{2}^{2}(16\log\lvert\mathcal{V}\rvert+9)+\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\left\|v\right\|^{2}_{2}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{32\log(2\delta^{-1})}{d}}+\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\left\|v\right\|^{4}_{2}\cdot\frac{64\log(2\delta^{-1})}{d}.

Let
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
and
f\in\mathcal{F}
. To bound
\lvert\left\langle\psi(s,a),w_{f}\right\rangle-\mathcal{T}f(s,a)\rvert
(cf.
Eq. (
30
)
), we apply the first bound of

Lemma

E.2
with
u=\left(P(s^{\prime}\mid s,a)/\mu^{1/2}(s^{\prime})\right)_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}
and
v=\left(\mu^{1/2}(s^{\prime})f(s^{\prime})\right)_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}
, which gives

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:northamericanscum}\left\lvert\left\langle\psi(s,a),w_{f}\right\rangle-\mathcal{T}f(s,a)\right\rvert\leq\sqrt{\frac{32C_{\texttt{push}}\log(2\deltainv)}{d}}+64C_{\texttt{push}}\frac{\log(2\deltainv)}{d}\coloneqq\varepsilon(\delta^{-1}),

(34)

where we have again used that
\left\|u\right\|^{2}_{2}=\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{P^{2}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a)}{\mu(s^{\prime})}\leq C_{\texttt{push}}
and also that
\left\|v\right\|^{2}_{2}=1
since
\|f\|_{\infty}\leq 1
for all
f\in\mathcal{F}
. To bound
Eq. (
31
)
, we apply the second bound of
Lemma

E.2
with
\mathcal{V}=\left\{\left(\frac{P_{h-1}(s^{\prime}\mid s,a)}{\mu^{1/2}_{h}(s^{\prime})}\right)_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}s,a\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\\
h\times[H]\end{subarray}}
, which gives

\max_{s,a\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A},h\in[H]}\left\|\psi_{h}(s,a)\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{\texttt{push}}(16\log\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert H+9)+C_{\texttt{push}}\sqrt{\frac{32\log(2\delta^{-1})}{d}}+C_{\texttt{push}}^{2}\frac{64\log(2\delta^{-1})}{d}\coloneqq B_{1}.

Lastly, to bound
Eq. (
32
)
, we take
\mathcal{V}=\left\{\left(\mu^{1/2}_{h}(s^{\prime})f_{h}(s^{\prime})\right)_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in\mathcal{F}\\
h\in[H]\end{subarray}}
in
Lemma

E.2
, which establishes that

\max_{f\in\mathcal{F},h\in[H]}\|w_{f,h}\|^{2}_{2}\leq 9+16\log\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert H+\sqrt{\frac{32\log(2\delta^{-1})}{d}}+\frac{64\log(2\delta^{-1})}{d}\coloneqq B_{2}.

Note that
Eq. (
34
)
establishes that the Bellman backup
\mathcal{T}f(s,a)
is well-approximated by
\langle\psi(s,a),w_{f}\rangle
only at a single state-action pair
(s,a)
. We can obtain an
L_{\infty}
-approximation guarantee by taking a union bound over
\mathcal{S}
and
\mathcal{A}
, which would incur a dependence on
\log\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert
in the final sample complexity. Here, we bypass this by instead requiring only an approximation guarantee under the
L_{2}(\mu\otimes\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{A}))
norm. Via (pushforward) coverability, this will ensure that
\En^{\pi}\left[\left(\langle w_{f},\psi(s,a)\rangle-\mathcal{T}f(s,a)\right)^{2}\right]
is well-controlled for all policies
\pi
, which will be sufficient for our downstream sample-complexity analysis of
Golf
. However, directly establishing an
L_{2}(\mu\otimes\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{A}))
approximation guarantee is technically challenging since it would require establishing a fourth-order (rather than second-order) equivalent of
Eq. (
33
)
.
The remainder of the proof will obtain an
L_{2}(\mu\otimes\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{A}))
approximation guarantee by instead sampling a dataset of size
n
from
\mu\otimes\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{A})
and taking a union bound over that dataset to ensure a uniform bound on all state-action pairs in that dataset. Via an additional concentration bound, this will ensure that the error is well-behaved under the
L_{2}(\mu\otimes\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{A}))
norm.

For each
h\in[H]
, sample a dataset
D=\{(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})\}_{i=1}^{n}
i.i.d. from
\mu_{h}\otimes\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{A})
. By a union bound over
n
,
\mathcal{F}
, and
H
, we have that

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:theparty}\forall i\in[n],f\in\mathcal{F},h\in[H]:\quad\left\lvert\left\langle\psi_{h}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h}),w_{f,h}\right\rangle-\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h+1}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon(n\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert H\delta^{-1}),

(35)

where we recall the definition of
\varepsilon(\cdot)
from
Eq. (
34
)
.
Now, let

X_{f,h}(s,a)\coloneqq\left(\texttt{clip}_{[0,2]}\left[\left\langle\psi_{h}(s,a),w_{f,h}\right\rangle\right]-\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h+1}(s,a)\right)^{2}.

Note that
\lvert X_{f,h}(s,a)\rvert\leq 4
and

X_{f,h}(s,a)\leq\left(\left\langle\psi_{h}(s,a),w_{f,h}\right\rangle-\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h+1}(s,a)\right)^{2},

since
\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h+1}(s,a)\in[0,2]
and the clipping operator is 1-Lipshitz. Note that

\En_{(s,a)\sim\mu_{h}\otimes\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{A})}\left[X_{f,h}(s,a)\right]\coloneqq\En_{\mu_{h}\otimes\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{A})}\left[\left(\texttt{clip}_{[0,2]}\left[\left\langle\psi_{h}(s,a),w_{f}\right\rangle\right]-\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h+1}(s,a)\right)^{2}\right],

where this expectation is only over the sampling of the data point
(s,a)
(and not the Rademacher matrix
W
).
Let

X_{i,f,h}\coloneqq X_{f,h}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h}).

By boundedness of
X_{f,h}(s,a)
and Hoeffding’s inequality, we have that with probability at least

1-\delta
:

\left\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i,f,h}-\En_{\mu\otimes\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{A})}\left[X_{f,h}(s,a)\right]\right\rvert\leq 4\sqrt{\frac{\log(2\deltainv)}{n}}.

Taking another union bound over
\mathcal{F}
and
H
as well as the event in
Eq. (
35
)
gives that

\displaystyle\forall f\in\mathcal{F},h\in[H]:\qquad

\displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i,f,h}-\En_{\mu\otimes\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{A})}\left[X_{f,h}(s,a)\right]\right\rvert\leq 4\sqrt{\frac{\log(2\abs{\cF}H\deltainv)}{n}},

(36)

\displaystyle\text{ and }\forall i\in[n],f\in\mathcal{F},h\in[H]:\qquad

\displaystyle X_{i,f,h}\leq\varepsilon^{2}(n\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert H\delta^{-1}),

(37)

recalling the definition of
\varepsilon(\cdot)
from
Eq. (
34
)
.
Then, re-arranging
Eq. (
36
)
gives us that

\displaystyle\En_{\mu\otimes\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{A})}\left[\left(\texttt{clip}_{[0,2]}[\left\langle\psi_{h}(s_{h},a_{h}),w_{f}\right\rangle]-\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h+1}(s_{h},a_{h})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i,f,h}+4\sqrt{\frac{\log(2\abs{\cF}H\deltainv)}{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{2}(n\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert H\delta^{-1})+4\sqrt{\frac{\log(2\abs{\cF}H\deltainv)}{n}},

(38)

We now conclude the proof by picking
n
and
d
appropriately to ensure that the right-hand-side is bounded by
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}
, which will ensure the desired claim that

\En_{\mu\otimes\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{A})}\left[\left(\texttt{clip}_{[0,2]}\left[\left\langle\psi_{h}(s_{h},a_{h}),w_{f}\right\rangle\right]-\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h+1}(s_{h},a_{h})\right)^{2}\right]\leq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}.

For convenience, we introduce absolute constants
c
and
c^{\prime}
whose precise values may change from line to line. We pick
n=64\log(2\abs{\cF}H\deltainv)/\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}^{2}
. Plugging this into
Eq.

38
gives

\displaystyle\En_{\mu\otimes\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{A})}\left[\left(\texttt{clip}_{[0,2]}\left[\left\langle\psi_{h}(s_{h},a_{h}),w_{f}\right\rangle\right]-\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h+1}(s_{h},a_{h})\right)^{2}\right]\leq\varepsilon^{2}(n\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert H\delta^{-1})+c\cdot\varepsilon

(39)

Noting that
n\leq 128\frac{\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert H\delta^{-1}}{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}^{2}}
and plugging this into
\varepsilon
(
Eq. (
34
)
) gives

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:sanctified}\varepsilon(n\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert H\delta^{-1})\leq C_{\texttt{push}}^{1/2}\sqrt{\frac{64\log(16\abs{\cF}H\deltainv/\vepsapx)}{d}}+C_{\texttt{push}}\frac{128\log(16\abs{\cF}H\deltainv/\vepsapx)}{d}.

(40)

Setting

d\geq 2^{9}\frac{C_{\texttt{push}}\log(16\abs{\cF}H\deltainv/\vepsapx)}{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}}

ensures that

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:sunglasses}\varepsilon^{2}(n\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert H\delta^{-1})\leq\varepsilon(n\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert H\delta^{-1})\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}}{2}

(41)

by
Eq. (
40
)
. Combining
Eq. (
38
)
and
Eq. (
41
)
, we get

\displaystyle\En_{\mu\otimes\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{A})}\left[\left(\texttt{clip}_{[0,2]}\left[\left\langle\psi_{h}(s_{h},a_{h}),w_{f}\right\rangle\right]-\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h+1}(s_{h},a_{h})\right)^{2}\right]\leq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}},

(42)

as desired. It only remains to establish the concentration results of
Lemma

E.2
. ∎

Proof of
Lemma

E.2
.

We establish the first claim. Let
i\in[d]
be fixed, and consider the random variable

\displaystyle Z_{i}\vcentcolon={}\sum_{j\in[n]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[n]\\
k\neq{}j\end{subarray}}W_{i,j}W_{i,k}v_{j}u_{k}.

Note that
\En\left[Z_{i}\right]=0
by independence of
W_{i,j}
and
W_{i,k}
for every
j\neq k
. By Exercise 6.9 of
[
BLM13
]
, we have that

\displaystyle\log\En\left[\exp\left(\lambda{}Z_{i}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq{}\frac{16\lambda^{2}}{2(1-64\|u\|_{2}^{2}\|v\|_{2}^{2}\lambda)}\|u\|_{2}^{2}\|v\|_{2}^{2}.

Since
Z_{i}
are independent, it follows that

\displaystyle\log\En\left[\exp\left(\lambda{}\sum_{i=1}^{d}Z_{i}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq{}\frac{16\lambda^{2}}{2(1-64\|u\|_{2}^{2}\|v\|_{2}^{2}\lambda)}\|u\|_{2}^{2}\|v\|_{2}^{2}d.

Hence,
\sum_{i=1}^{d}Z_{i}
is a sub-Gamma random variable with parameters

\nu=16\left\|u\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|v\right\|_{2}^{2}d
and

c=64\left\|u\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|v\right\|_{2}^{2}
, and it follows from Equation (2.5) on page 29 of

[
BLM13
]
that for all
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}Z_{i}\geq{}\|u\|_{2}\|v\|_{2}\sqrt{32d\varepsilon}+64\left\|u\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|v\right\|_{2}^{2}\varepsilon\right)\leq{}e^{-\varepsilon}.

Taking a union bound, and using that the random variable is symmetric, we obtain the desired claim.

We now establish the second claim. Let
\mathcal{V}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a subset of vectors. Let
i\in[d]
be fixed, and re-consider the random variable

\displaystyle Z_{i}\vcentcolon={}\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\sum_{j\in[n]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[n]\\
k\neq j\end{subarray}}W_{i,j}W_{i,k}v_{j}v_{k}.

Again appealing to Exercise 6.9 of
[
BLM13
]
, we have that

\displaystyle\log\En\left[\exp\left(\lambda{}\left(Z_{i}-\En[Z_{i}]\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq{}\frac{16\lambda^{2}}{2(1-64B\lambda)}\En\left[\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\sum_{j\in[n]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[n]\\
k\neq j\end{subarray}}W_{i,j}W_{i,k}v^{2}_{j}v^{2}_{k}\right]

\displaystyle\leq{}\frac{16\lambda^{2}}{2(1-64B\lambda)}\En\left[\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\sum_{j,k=1}^{n}v^{2}_{j}v^{2}_{k}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{16\lambda^{2}}{2(1-64B\lambda)}\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\left\|v\right\|_{2}^{4}

where
B\vcentcolon=\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\|v\|_{2}^{4}
.
Since
Z_{i}
are independent, it follows that

\displaystyle\log\En\left[\exp\left(\lambda{}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(Z_{i}-\En[Z_{i}]\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq{}\frac{16\lambda^{2}}{2(1-64B\lambda)}\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\left\|v\right\|_{2}^{4}d.

Hence,
\sum_{i=1}^{d}Z_{i}
is a sub-Gamma random variable with parameters

\nu=16\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\left\|v\right\|_{2}^{4}d
and

c=64\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\left\|v\right\|_{2}^{4}
, and it follows from Equation (2.5) on page 29 of

[
BLM13
]
that for all
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}Z_{i}\geq{}\En[Z_{i}]+\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\left\|v\right\|^{2}_{4}\sqrt{\frac{32\varepsilon}{d}}+64\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\left\|v\right\|_{2}^{4}\frac{\varepsilon}{d}\right)\leq{}e^{-\varepsilon}.

To conclude, it remains only to show the bound
\En[Z_{i}]\leq\max_{v}\|v\|_{2}^{2}(16\log\lvert\mathcal{V}\rvert+9)
. This follows by a standard log-sum-exp approach. Below, we abbreviate
\rho_{j}\coloneqq W_{i,j}
. We can observe that for any
\lambda>0
:

\displaystyle\En[Z_{i}]

\displaystyle=\En\left[\max_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\sum_{j\in[n]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[n]\\
k\neq j\end{subarray}}\rho_{j}\rho_{k}v_{j}v_{k}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\log\left(\sum_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\En\left[\exp(\lambda\sum_{j \in[n]} \sum_{\substack{k \in[n] \\ k \neq j}} \rho_j \rho_k v_j v_k)\right]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\log\left(\sum_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\En\left[\exp(\lambda\prn*{\sum_{j=1}^n \rho_j v_j}^2)\right]\right)

(43)

Note that
X\coloneqq\sum_{j}\rho_{j}v_{j}
is subGaussian with parameter
\|v\|_{2}^{2}
, since:

\En\left[\exp(\lambda\sum_{j=1}^n \rho_j v_j )\right]=\prod_{j=1}^{n}\En\left[\exp(\lambda\rho_j v_j)\right]\leq\prod_{j=1}^{n}\exp(\frac{\lambda^2 v^2_j}{2})=\exp(\frac{\lambda^2}{2} \nrm{v}_2^2).

Then, it follows (e.g. Lemma 1.12 of
[
RH23
]
) that
X^{2}-\En[X^{2}]
satisfies a sub-exponential MGF bound with parameter
16\|v\|_{2}^{2}
, i.e.

\En[\lambda(X^{2}-\exp(\lambda(X^2 - \En\brk{X^2}))[X^{2}])]\leq\exp(\frac{256}{2}\lambda^2 \nrm{v}_2^4)\qquad\forall\lvert\lambda\rvert\leq\frac{1}{16\|v\|_{2}^{2}}.

We also note that

\En[X^{2}]=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}v_{i}v_{j}\En[\varepsilon_{i}\varepsilon_{j}]=\|v\|_{2}^{2}.

Adding and subtracting
\En[X^{2}]
in
Eq. (
43
)
gives

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\log\left(\sum_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\En\left[\exp(\lambda\prn*{X^2 - \nrm{v}_2^2} + \lambda\nrm{v}_2^2)\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda}\log\left(\sum_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\En\left[\exp(\lambda\prn*{X^2 - \nrm{v}_2^2})\right]\exp(\lambda\nrm{v}_2^2)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\log\left(\sum_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\exp(128\lambda^2\nrm{v}_2^4 + \lambda\nrm{v}_2^2)\right)\qquad\forall\lvert\lambda\rvert\leq\frac{1}{16\max_{v}\|v\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\log\lvert\mathcal{V}\rvert+\max_{v}128\lambda\|v\|_{2}^{4}+\max_{v}\|v\|_{2}^{2}\qquad\forall\lvert\lambda\rvert\leq\frac{1}{16\max_{v}\|v\|_{2}^{2}}

Picking
\lambda=\frac{1}{16\max_{v}\|v\|_{2}^{2}}
concludes the proof.
∎

E.1.2
Golf
with on-policy misspecification

Consider the version of
Golf

[
JLM21
]
in

Algorithm

2
. We have the following guarantee for the regret of

Golf
, which extends
[
JLM21
]
to allow for
on-policy
misspecification.

Algorithm 2

GOLF

[
JLM21
]

input:
Function classes
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
, confidence width
\beta>0
.

initialize:

\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(0)}}\leftarrow\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{D}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(0)}}\leftarrow\emptyset\;\;\forall h\in[H]
.

1:

for
episode
t=1,2,\dotsc,T

do

2:

Select policy
\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}\leftarrow\pi_{f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}
, where
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}\vcentcolon={}\argmax_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t-1)}}}f(x_{1},\pi_{f,1}(x_{1}))
.

3:

Execute
\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}
for one episode and obtain trajectory
(x_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},a_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},r_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}),\ldots,(x_{H}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},a_{H}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},r_{H}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}})
.

4:

Update dataset:
\mathcal{D}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}\leftarrow\mathcal{D}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t-1)}}\cup\big\{\big(x_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},a_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},x_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}\big)\big\}\;\;\forall h\in[H]
.

5:

Compute confidence set:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}\leftarrow\bigg\{f\in\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{L}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}(f_{h},f_{h+1})-\min_{g_{h}\in\mathcal{G}_{h}}\mathcal{L}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}(g_{h},f_{h+1})\leq\beta\;\;\forall h\in[H]\bigg\},

\displaystyle\text{where \quad}\mathcal{L}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}(f,f^{\prime})\coloneqq\sum_{(x,a,r,x^{\prime})\in\mathcal{D}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\Big(f(x,a)-r-\max_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}f^{\prime}(x^{\prime},a^{\prime})\Big)^{2},\penalty\ \forall f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}.

6:

end

for

7:

Output
\widehat{\pi}=\texttt{Unif}(\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(1:T)}})
.

Lemma E.3
.

Suppose that
Q^{M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}},\star}\in\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
satisfies

\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}
-completeness in the sense that for all
h\in[H]
and
f\in\mathcal{F}_{h+1}
,
there exists
g\in\mathcal{G}_{h}
such that

\En^{\pi}\left(g-\mathcal{T}^{M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}}_{h}f\right)^{2}\leq{}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}^{2}
for all
\pi\in\Pi_{\mathcal{F}}\coloneqq\left\{\pi_{f}:f\in\mathcal{F}\right\}
. Let
C_{\texttt{cov}}\coloneqq C_{\texttt{cov}}(M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}},\Pi_{\mathcal{F}})
(
Definition

C.3
). Then for an
appropriate choice of
\beta
,
Algorithm

2
ensures that

\displaystyle\texttt{Reg}\leq{}H\sqrt{C_{\texttt{cov}}{}T\log(\abs{\cF}\abs{\cG}HT/\delta)}+HT\sqrt{C_{\texttt{cov}}\log(T)}\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}.

Proof of
Lemma

E.3
.
For each
f_{h+1}\in\mathcal{F}_{h+1}
, let
\mathsf{apx}[f_{h}]=\argmin_{g_{h}\in\mathcal{G}_{h}}\sup_{\pi\in\Pi}\En^{\pi}\left[\left(g_{h}-\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h+1}\right)^{2}\right]
. Let

\delta^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot)\coloneqq f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot)-\mathcal{T}_{f}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h+1}(\cdot,\cdot)\quad\&\quad\widetilde{\delta}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot)\coloneqq f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot)-\mathsf{apx}\left[f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h+1}\right](\cdot,\cdot),

and note that by Jensen’s inequality we have that for all
\pi
,
\En^{\pi}\left[\delta^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot)\right]\leq\En^{\pi}\left[\widetilde{\delta}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot)\right]+\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}
. We further adopt the shorthand
d^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x,a)\coloneqq d^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}_{h}(x,a)
and
\tilde{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x,a)\coloneqq\sum_{i<t}d^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x,a)
.
As a consequence of realizability (
Q^{\star}_{\texttt{obs},h}\in\mathcal{F}_{h}
) and
approximate Bellman completeness, standard concentration arguments
(proved in the sequel) lead to the following result.

Lemma E.4

(Optimism and small in-sample squared Bellman errors)
.

With probability at least
1-\delta
, by taking
\beta=c\log(TH\abs{\cF}\abs{\cG}/\delta)+T\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}
, we have that for all
t\in[T]
,

(i)\,\,Q^{\star}_{\texttt{obs},h}\in\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},\quad\text{and}\quad(ii)\,\,\sum_{x,a}\tilde{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x,a)\left(\widetilde{\delta}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x,a)\right)^{2}\leq\mathcal{O}(\beta).

The rest of the proof proceeds similarly to the analysis of Section
3.2 in
[
XFBJK23
]
. Namely, by optimism (
Lemma

E.4
) and a standard Bellman error decomposition (
Lemma

B.6
) we have

\texttt{Reg}\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En_{d^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}}\left[\delta^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x,a)\right]\leq TH\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En_{d^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}}\left[\widetilde{\delta}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x,a)\right].

Let us defining the burn-in time

\tau_{h}(x,a)=\min\{t\mid\tilde{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x,a)\geq C_{\texttt{cov}}\mu^{\star}_{h}(x,a)\},

where
\mu^{\star}_{h}
is the coverability distribution for the set of
policies
\Pi_{\mathcal{F}}
(i.e., the distribution
\mu^{\star}_{h}
that achieves
the minimum in the coverability definition). Using the same decomposition into “burn-in
phase” and “stable phase” in
[
XFBJK23
]
, we have:

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En_{d^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}}\left[\widetilde{\delta}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x,a)\right]\leq 2HC_{\texttt{cov}}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En_{d^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}}\left[\widetilde{\delta}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x,a)\mathbb{I}\left\{t\geq\tau_{h}(x,a)\right\}\right].

Applying a change of measure argument on the second term then gives:

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En_{d^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}}\left[\widetilde{\delta}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x,a)\mathbb{I}\left\{t\geq\tau_{h}(x,a)\right\}\right]\leq H\underbrace{\sqrt{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{x,a}\frac{\left(\mathbb{I}\{t\geq\tau_{h}(x,a)\}d^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x,a)\right)^{2}}{\tilde{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x,a)}}}_{\texttt{(A)}}\underbrace{\sqrt{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{x,a}\tilde{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x,a)\left(\widetilde{\delta}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x,a)\right)^{2}}}_{\texttt{(B)}}

By the same reasoning as in
[
XFBJK23
]
, we have
\texttt{(A)}\leq\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{C_{\texttt{cov}}\log(T)})
, and by
Lemma

E.4
we have
\texttt{(B)}\leq\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\beta T})
. Using that
\beta=\log(TH\abs{\cF}/\delta)+T\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}^{2}
gives the desired result. It remains to establish the concentration results of
Lemma

E.4
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

E.4
.

For any function
f
, define a random variable

X_{t}(h,f)=\left(f_{h}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})-r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}-f_{h+1}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h+1})\right)^{2}-\left(\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h+1}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})-r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}-f_{h+1}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h+1})\right)^{2}.

Let
\mathfrak{F}_{t,h}=\{s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{1},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{1},r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{1},\ldots,s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{H},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{H},r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{H}\}_{i<t}
. Note that

\displaystyle\En\left[r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}+f_{h+1}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h+1})\mid\mathfrak{F}_{t,h}\right]=\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[\mathcal{T}_{h}f(s_{h},a_{h})\right].

(44)

and thus that

\En\left[X_{t}(h,f)\mid\mathfrak{F}_{t,h}\right]=\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[\left(f_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})-\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})\right)^{2}\right].

Next, note that

\displaystyle\texttt{Var}\left[X_{t}(h,f)\mid\mathfrak{F}_{t,h}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\En\left[\left(X_{t}(h,f)\right)^{2}\mid\mathfrak{F}_{t,h}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip-30.00005pt\leq\En\left[\left(f_{h}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})-\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})\right)^{2}\left(f_{h}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})+\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})+2\left(r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}-f_{h+1}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h+1})\right)\right)^{2}\mid\mathfrak{F}_{t,h}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip-30.00005pt\leq 16\En\left[\left(f_{h}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})-\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})\right)^{2}\mid\mathfrak{F}_{t,h}\right]=16\En\left[X_{t}(h,f)\mid\mathfrak{F}_{t,h}\right].

By Freedman’s inequality (
Lemma

B.2
,
Lemma

B.3
), we have that with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\left\lvert\sum_{i<t}X_{i}(h,f)-\sum_{i<t}\En\left[X_{i}(h,f)\mid\mathfrak{F}_{i,h}\right]\right\rvert\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)\sum_{i<t}\En\left[X_{i}(h,f)\mid\mathfrak{F}_{i,h}\right]}+\log(1/\delta)\right)

Taking a union bound over
[T]\times[H]\times\mathcal{F}
, we have
that for all
t,h,f
, with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:x-t-minus-bellman-errors-sec3}\left\lvert\sum_{i<t}X_{i}(h,f)-\sum_{i<t}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}\left[\left(f_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})-\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})\right)^{2}\right]\right\rvert\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\iota\sum_{i<t}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}\left[\left(f_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})-\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})\right)^{2}\right]}+\iota\right),

(45)

where
\iota=\log(\abs*{\cF}HT/\delta)
. We now show that

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:latent-golf-x-t-bounded-sec3}\sum_{i<t}X_{i}(h,f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}})\leq\beta+\mathcal{O}\left(T\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}^{2}+\iota\right)=\mathcal{O}\left(\beta\right),

(46)

which will imply, from
Eq. (
45
)
, that

\sum_{i<t}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[\left(f_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})-\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})\right)^{2}\right]\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\iota+\beta\right)=\mathcal{O}(\beta),

as desired. To see
Eq. (
46
)
, let

\Delta_{t}=\sum_{i<t}\left(\mathsf{apx}\left[\mathcal{T}_{h}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h+1}\right](s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})-r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h+1}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})\right)^{2}-\left(\mathcal{T}_{h}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})-r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h+1}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})\right)^{2}

and then note that:

\displaystyle\sum_{i<t}X_{i}(h,f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i<t}\left(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})-r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h+1}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})\right)^{2}-\left(\mathcal{T}_{h}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})-r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h+1}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i<t}\left(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})-r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})\right)^{2}-\left(\mathsf{apx}\left[\mathcal{T}_{h}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h+1}\right](s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})-r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h+1}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})\right)^{2}+\Delta_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i<t}\left(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})-r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h+1}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})\right)^{2}-\inf_{g_{h}\in\mathcal{G}_{h}}\sum_{i<t}\left(g(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})-r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h+1}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})\right)^{2}+\Delta_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\beta+\Delta_{t}.

where the second-to-last line follows from
\mathsf{apx}\left[\mathcal{T}_{h}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h+1}\right]\in\mathcal{G}
and the last line follows from the definition of the confidence set. It remains to show that
\Delta_{t}\leq\mathcal{O}(T\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}^{2}+\iota)
, which we do via a similar concentration argument. Namely, let

Y_{t}(h,f)=\left(\mathsf{apx}\left[\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h+1}\right](s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})-r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}_{h+1}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h+1})\right)^{2}-\left(\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})-r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}_{h+1}(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h+1})\right)^{2},

and note that, as before,

\En\left[Y_{t}(h,f)\mid\mathfrak{F}_{t,h}\right]=\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[\left(\mathsf{apx}\left[\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h+1}\right](s_{h},a_{h})-\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})\right)^{2}\right],

and

\texttt{Var}\left[Y_{t}(h,f)\mid\mathfrak{F}_{t,h}\right]\leq 16\En\left[Y_{t}(h,f)\mid\mathfrak{F}_{t,h}\right],

by the same calculation as earlier.
Thus, by Freedman’s inequality and a union bound, we have that, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\sum_{i<t}Y_{t}(h,f)-\sum_{i<t}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[\left(\mathsf{apx}\left[\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h+1}\right](s_{h},a_{h})-\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})\right)^{2}\right]\right\rvert

(47)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\iota\sum_{i<t}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[\left(\mathsf{apx}\left[\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h+1}\right](s_{h},a_{h})-\mathcal{T}_{h}f_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})\right)^{2}\right]}+\iota\right),

(48)

where
\iota=\log(\abs*{\cF}HT/\delta)
. Recalling the misspecification assumption, this implies that

\sum_{i<t}Y_{t}(h,f)\leq\mathcal{O}\left(t\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}^{2}+\iota\right),

for all
h,f,t
, with high probability. This concludes the result for
(ii)
. For
(i)
, this follows identically to the proof of Lemma 40 in
[
JLM21
]
, since this only uses the property that
Q^{\star}\in\mathcal{F}
.

∎

E.1.3
Sample-efficient latent-dynamics RL under pushforward coverability

We conclude by combining the previous two results to obtain the main result for this section.

See
3.2

Proof of
Theorem

3.2
.
Let
M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}\coloneqq\left\llangle M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}},\psi^{\star}\right\rrangle\in\left\llangle\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\Phi\right\rrangle
be the unknown latent-dynamics MDP. Define observation-level value functions

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}=\left\{Q^{{\scriptscriptstyle M_{\texttt{lat}}},\star}\circ\phi\mid{}M_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}},\phi\in\Phi\right\},

so that
Q^{M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}},\star}=Q^{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}},\star}\circ\phi^{\star}\in\mathcal{F}
via decoder and model realizability, and

\log\lvert\mathcal{F}_{h}\rvert\leq{}\log\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert\lvert\Phi\rvert
. Consider any function class
\mathcal{L}\subseteq\{\mathcal{S}\to[0,1]\}
and MDP
M_{\texttt{lat}}=(r_{\texttt{lat}},P_{\texttt{lat}})
. For a given value
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}>0
, setting
d
according to
Lemma

E.1
implies that there exists a
d
-dimensional feature map
\varphi_{{\scriptscriptstyle M_{\texttt{lat}}},h}(s,a)\in\mathbb{R}^{d+1}
such that for all
\ell\in\mathcal{L}
and
h\in[H]
,
there exists
w_{\ell,h}\in\mathbb{R}^{d+1}
such that

\displaystyle\En_{\mu_{M_{\texttt{lat}}}\otimes\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{A})}\left[\left(\texttt{clip}_{[0,2]}\left[\left\langle\varphi_{{\scriptscriptstyle M_{\texttt{lat}}},h}(s,a),w_{\ell,h}\right\rangle\right]-\mathcal{T}^{M_{\texttt{lat}}}_{h}\ell_{h+1}(s,a)\right)^{2}\right]\leq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}},

(49)

where
\mu_{M_{\texttt{lat}}}
is the pushforward coverability distribution for
M_{\texttt{lat}}
.
Moreover, the map
\varphi_{h}
is explicitly computed as a function of

M_{\texttt{lat}}
by a randomized algorithm with success probability
1-\delta
, with no knowledge of the class
\mathcal{L}
required. We
consider the class

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=\left\{\Gamma_{\phi}\circ Q^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\star}(s,a)\coloneqq\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\Gamma_{\phi}(s^{\prime}\mid s)Q^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\star}(s^{\prime},a)\mid\phi\in\Phi,M_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\right\},

(50)

where
\Gamma_{\phi}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{S})
is the mismatch function for decoder
\phi
and emission
\psi^{\star}
, defined in
Definition

3.4
. Note that
\mathcal{L}
has size
\log\left\lvert\mathcal{L}\right\rvert\leq\log\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert\lvert\Phi\rvert
, and that we have

\mathcal{T}^{M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}}_{h}(Q^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\star}_{h}\circ\phi_{h})(x,a)=\mathcal{T}^{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}}_{h}(\Gamma_{\phi,h+1}\circ V^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\star}_{h})(\phi^{\star}_{h}(x),a)

by
Lemma

C.7
.
By
Lemma

C.1
we have that
\mu_{M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}},h}(x)=\psi^{\star}_{h}(x\mid\phi^{\star}_{h}(x))\mu_{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}},h}(\phi^{\star}_{h}(x))
is the coverability distribution for MDP
M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}
, and

\En_{\mu_{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}}\otimes\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{A})}\left[f(s,a)\right]=\En_{\mu_{M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}}\otimes\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{A})}\left[f(\phi^{\star}(x),a)\right].

Now, define

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{{\scriptscriptstyle M_{\texttt{lat}}},h}=\left\{(x,a)\mapsto{}\texttt{clip}_{[0,2]}\left[\left\langle\varphi_{M_{\texttt{lat}},h}(\phi(x),a),w\right\rangle\right]\mid{}\phi\in\Phi,\left\|w\right\|^{2}_{2}\leq{}11+16\log(\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert\lvert\Phi\rvert H)\right\}.

Recall the definition of
w_{f}
(for
f:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\rightarrow[0,1]
) from
Lemma

E.1
, and note that by the norm bound
\max_{\ell\in\mathcal{L}}\|w_{\ell}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 11+16\log(\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert\lvert\Phi\rvert H)
given by
Lemma

E.1
, we have
(x,a)\mapsto{}\left\langle\varphi_{M_{\texttt{lat}},h}(\phi(x),a),w_{\ell}\right\rangle\in\mathcal{G}_{h}
for every
\ell\in\mathcal{L}
. Next, note that by the norm bound
\max_{s,a}\|\psi(s,a)\|^{2}_{2}\leq C_{\texttt{push}}(11+16\log(\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert H))
, given by
Lemma

E.1
, we have every
g_{h}\in\mathcal{G}_{M_{\texttt{lat}},h}
satisfies
\|g_{h}\|_{\infty}\leq cC_{\texttt{push}}^{1/2}\log\left(\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert\lvert\Phi\rvert\lvert\mathcal{S}\rvert\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert H\right)\coloneqq B
for some absolute constant
c
. Therefore,
\mathcal{G}_{M_{\texttt{lat}},h}
has size
\log\lvert\mathcal{G}_{M_{\texttt{lat}},h}\rvert\leq\widetilde{O}\left(d\cdot\log(B)+\log\lvert\Phi\rvert\right)=\widetilde{O}\left(d\log\log(\abs{\cS})+\log\lvert\Phi\rvert\right)
, where the
\widetilde{O}
notation ignores logarithmic factors of
C_{\texttt{push}}
,
\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert
,
\log\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert
, and
\log\lvert\Phi\rvert
.
20
20

20

Formally, this requires a standard covering number argument; we omit the details.
Define
\mathcal{G}_{h}=\cup_{M_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}}\mathcal{G}_{M_{\texttt{lat}},h}
, which has size
\log\lvert\mathcal{G}_{h}\rvert\leq\log\lvert M_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert+(\widetilde{O}\left(d\log\log(\abs{\cS})+\log\lvert\Phi\rvert\right))
. Together, these results with
Lemma

E.1
imply that for all
f_{h+1}\in\mathcal{F}_{h+1}
, there exists
g_{h}\in\mathcal{G}_{h}
such that

\displaystyle\En_{\mu_{M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}},h}\otimes\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{A})}\left[\left(g_{h}(x_{h},a_{h})-\left[\mathcal{T}^{M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}}_{h}f_{h+1}\right](x_{h},a_{h})\right)^{2}\right]\leq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}.

This, in turn, implies that for all
{\pi_{\texttt{obs}}}\in\Pi_{\texttt{rns}}
we have

\displaystyle\En^{{\pi_{\texttt{obs}}}}\left[\left(g_{h}(x_{h},a_{h})-\left[\mathcal{T}^{{\scriptscriptstyle M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}}}_{h}f_{h+1}\right](x_{h},a_{h})\right)^{2}\right]\leq C_{\texttt{push}}\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}},

since
\mu_{M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}},h}\otimes\texttt{Unif}(\mathcal{A})
satisfies coverability (
Definition

C.3
) with parameter
C_{\texttt{cov}}(M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}},\Pi_{\texttt{rns}})\leq C_{\texttt{push}}\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert
(
Eq. (
29
)
).

Then, it follows by
Lemma

E.3
that if we run

Algorithm

2
with the classes
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
we will get

Reg

\displaystyle\leq{}H\sqrt{C_{\texttt{push}}\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert{}T\log(\abs{\cMlat}\abs{\Phi}HT/\delta)(d\log\log(\abs{\cS})+\log\lvert\Phi\rvert)}+HT\sqrt{C_{\texttt{push}}^{2}\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert^{2}\log(T)\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}}

\displaystyle\leq{}H\sqrt{C_{\texttt{push}}^{5}\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert{}T\log(\abs{\cMlat}\abs{\Phi}HT/\delta)\frac{\log(\Cpush^2\abs{\cMlat}\abs{\Phi}^2H\deltainv/\vepsapx)\log\log(\abs{\cS})}{\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}}}+HT\sqrt{C_{\texttt{push}}^{2}\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert^{2}\log(T)\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}}

Choosing
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{apx}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}
to balance leads to

Reg

\displaystyle\lesssim{}HT^{3/4}\sqrt{C_{\texttt{push}}^{5}\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert{}\log(\abs{\cMlat}\abs{\Phi}HT/\delta)\log(\Cpush^2\abs{\cMlat}\abs{\Phi}^2H\deltainv T)\log\log(\abs{\cS})}+HT^{3/4}\sqrt{C_{\texttt{push}}^{2}\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert^{2}\log(T)}

\displaystyle\lesssim HT^{3/4}\sqrt{C_{\texttt{push}}^{5}\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert^{2}\log(\abs{\cMlat}\abs{\Phi}HT/\delta)\log(T\Cpush^2\abs{\cMlat}\abs{\Phi}^2H/\delta)\log\log(\abs{\cS})},

which gives a risk bound of

Risk

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{T^{1/4}}H\sqrt{C_{\texttt{push}}^{5}\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert^{2}\log(\abs{\cMlat}\abs{\Phi}HT/\delta)\log(T\Cpush^2\abs{\cMlat}\abs{\Phi}^2H/\delta)\log\log(\abs{\cS})}.

Equating this to
\varepsilon
gives a sample complexity of

T=\poly(C_{\texttt{push}},A,H,\log\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert,\log\lvert\Phi\rvert,\varepsilon^{-1},\log(\delta^{-1}),\log\log(\abs{\cS})),

as desired.
We have not made much effort to optimize the rate; in particular, a faster rate is possible by using the
Golf.Dbr
algorithm of
[
ACK24
]
, which improves over the
Golf
algorithm when there is misspecification.
∎
