F.1
Pseudocode and Proofs for
ExpWeights.Dr
(
Lemma

4.1
)

input
: Decoder set
\Phi

for

t=1,2,\cdots,T

do

Get dataset
\left\{x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},\phi^{\star}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})\right\}_{i\in[t-1],h\in[H]}

for

h=1,\ldots,H

do

For
\phi\in\Phi
, compute

q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(\phi_{h})\propto\exp\left(-\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\mathbb{I}\left[\phi_{h}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})\neq\phi^{\star}_{h}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})\right]\right),

and set

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:derandom-expweights}\bar{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x)=\argmax_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}_{\phi_{h}\sim q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}}(\phi_{h}(x)=s).

(51)

end

for

Return
\bar{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}=\{\bar{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{H}
.

end

for

Algorithm 3

Derandomized Exponential Weights (
ExpWeights.Dr
)

The main result for this estimator is the following.

See
4.1

Proof of
Lemma

4.1
.

For each
h\in[H]
, consider the realizable online classification problem where
x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}\sim d^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}_{h}
, for
\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}
chosen adversarially, and
y^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}=\phi^{\star}_{h}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})
. Consider the exponential weights estimator

q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(\phi)\propto\exp\left(-\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\mathbb{I}\left[\phi(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})\neq\phi^{\star}_{h}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})\right]\right).

For every sequence
(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})_{t=1}^{T}
, these distributions satisfy the deterministic regret bound

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\En_{\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}\sim q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}}\left[\mathbb{I}\left[\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})\neq\phi^{\star}_{h}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})\right]\right]\leq 2\log|\Phi|,

by Corollary 2.3 of
[
CBL06
]
. Taking conditional expectations over
x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}\sim d^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}_{h}
and using
Lemma

B.3

gives that with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\En_{\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}\sim q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[\mathbb{I}\left[\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x_{h})\neq\phi^{\star}_{h}(x_{h})\right]\right]\leq 4\log|\Phi|+8\log(2\deltainv).

Taking a union bound over
h\in[H]
and summing over
h\in[H]
we obtain that with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En_{\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}\sim q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[\mathbb{I}\left[\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x_{h})\neq\phi^{\star}_{h}(x_{h})\right]\right]\leq 4H\log|\Phi|+8H\log(2H\deltainv).

Now, recall that at each time
t
, we define the improper decoder
\bar{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}
via:

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:mode-feature}\bar{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x)=\argmax_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}_{\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}\sim q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}}(\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x)=s)

(52)

Let
\ell_{h}(x_{h},q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})=\mathbb{P}_{\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}\sim q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}}(\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x_{h})\neq\phi^{\star}_{h}(x_{h}))
. Note that
\ell
satisfies

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En_{\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}\sim q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[\mathbb{I}\left[\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x_{h})\neq\phi^{\star}_{h}(x_{h})\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\En_{\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}\sim q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}}\left[\mathbb{I}\left[\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x_{h})\neq\phi^{\star}_{h}(x_{h})\right]\right]

(53)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}[\ell_{h}(x_{h},q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})].

(54)

By abuse of notation we also denote
\ell_{h}(x_{h},\bar{\phi}_{h})=\mathbb{I}\left[\bar{\phi}_{h}(x)\neq\phi^{\star}(x)\right]
. We will show that

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:boop}\forall x,t,h:\ell_{h}(x_{h},\bar{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})\leq 2\ell_{h}(x_{h},q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}),

(55)

from which we will obtain that with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\texttt{Reg}_{\texttt{class}}(T)=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[\mathbb{I}\left[\bar{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x_{h})\neq\phi^{\star}_{h}(x_{h})\right]\right]\leq 8H\log|\Phi|+16H\log(2H\deltainv).

Integrating the high-probability regret bound gives

\En\left[\texttt{Reg}_{\texttt{class}}(T)\right]=\mathcal{O}\left(H\log(H|\Phi|)\right),

as desired.
Towards establishing
Eq. (
55
)
, let us fix
x
and let
s_{\max}
denote the argmax in
Eq. (
52
)
. There are two cases:

•

\mathbb{P}_{\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}\sim q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}}(\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x)=s_{\max})\geq\frac{1}{2}
:

\rightarrow

If
s_{\max}=\phi^{\star}(x)
,
\ell(x,\bar{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})=0
so we are done.

\rightarrow

Otherwise,
s_{\max}\neq\phi^{\star}(x)
and we have
\ell(x,\bar{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})=1
. However, since
\phi^{\star}(x)\neq s_{\max}
we have
\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x)=s_{\max}\implies\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x)\neq\phi^{\star}_{h}(x)
and so

\mathbb{P}_{\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}\sim q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}}(\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x)\neq\phi^{\star}_{h}(x))\geq\mathbb{P}_{\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}\sim q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}}(\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x)=s_{\max})\geq\frac{1}{2}=\frac{1}{2}\ell(x,\bar{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}).

•

\mathbb{P}_{\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}\sim q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}}(\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x)=s_{\max})<\frac{1}{2}
:

\rightarrow

If
s_{\max}=\phi^{\star}_{h}(x)
,
\ell(x,\bar{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})=0
so we are done.

\rightarrow

Otherwise,
s_{\max}\neq\phi^{\star}(x)
and we have
\ell(x,\bar{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})=1
. However, by definition of
s_{\max}
as the mode we also have

\mathbb{P}_{\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}\sim q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}}(\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x)=\phi^{\star}_{h}(x))\leq\mathbb{P}_{\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}\sim q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}}(\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x)=s_{\max})<\frac{1}{2},

so in particular we have

\ell(x,q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h})=\mathbb{P}_{\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}\sim q^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}}(\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x)\neq\phi^{\star}_{h}(x))>\frac{1}{2}=\frac{1}{2}\ell(x,\bar{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}).

∎

F.2
Proofs for
O2L
Under Hindsight Observability (
Theorem

4.1
)

See
4.1

Proof of
Theorem

4.1
.

Let
(\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}})_{t\in[T]}
denote the decoders chosen by
Rep
{}_{\texttt{class}}
, and let
\rho^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}
denote the distribution over decoders induced at time
t
from the interaction of
\textnormal{{Rep${}_{\texttt{class}}$}},\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}},
and
M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}
. Let
\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{\texttt{obs}}\coloneqq\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}
and
p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{\texttt{obs}}
denote the distribution over (observation-space) policies played at epoch
t
and episode
k
, induced by the interaction of
\textnormal{{Rep${}_{\texttt{class}}$}},\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}}
, and
M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}
. We adopt the notation
\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,K+1)}}_{\texttt{lat}}\coloneqq\widehat{\pi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{\texttt{lat}}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,K+1)}}_{\texttt{lat}}
for the final policy output by
Alg
{}_{\texttt{lat}}
in epoch
t
and
(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,K+1)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,K+1)}}_{h},r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,K+1)}}_{h})
for the trajectory collected from that (observation-level) policy
\widehat{\pi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}
.
We firstly note that by assumption, we have the guarantee

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:teebs}\En\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{k=1}^{K+1}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En_{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{\texttt{obs}}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{\texttt{obs}}}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{\texttt{obs}}}\left[\mathbb{I}\left[\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x_{h})\neq\phi^{\star}_{h}(x_{h})\right]\right]\right]\leq(K+1)\texttt{Est}_{\texttt{class}}(T)\leq 2K\texttt{Est}_{\texttt{class}}(T).

(56)

which follows by applying
Assumption

4.2
to the distributions
\bar{p}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{\texttt{obs}}=\frac{1}{(K+1)}\sum_{k=1}^{K+1}p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{\texttt{obs}}
and noting that

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\frac{1}{K+1}\sum_{k=1}^{K+1}\En_{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{\texttt{obs}}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{\texttt{obs}}}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{\texttt{obs}}}\left[\mathbb{I}\left[\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x_{h})\neq\phi^{\star}_{h}(x_{h})\right]\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En_{\bar{\pi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{\texttt{obs}}\sim\bar{p}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{\texttt{obs}}}\En^{\bar{\pi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{\texttt{obs}}}\left[\mathbb{I}\left[\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x_{h})\neq\phi^{\star}_{h}(x_{h})\right]\right]\leq\texttt{Est}_{\texttt{class}}(T).

Let
\texttt{Risk}(K,\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}},\phi,M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}})=J^{M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}}(\pi^{\star}_{M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}})-J^{M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}}(\widehat{\pi}_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\phi)
be the random variable denoting the risk of the final policy output by
Alg
{}_{\texttt{lat}}
after
K
rounds of interaction with
M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}
when given feature
\phi
in any epoch
t
.
For any
\phi:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}
, let
\En_{\phi}
denote the law over trajectories
(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}_{h},r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}_{h})_{k\in[K+1],h\in[H]}
and policies
(\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\phi)_{k\in[K+1]}
generated after
K
rounds of interaction when
Alg
{}_{\texttt{lat}}
is given feature
\phi
in any epoch. (Recall that, for all of the above definitions, a new instance of
Alg
{}_{\texttt{lat}}
is initialized at every epoch, so we do not have to specify
which epoch it is
, only the current feature
\phi
). Finally, let
G_{t}
be the “good” event

G_{t}=\left\{\forall k\in[K+1],\forall h\in[H]:\,\,\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{h})=\phi^{\star}_{h}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{h})\right\}.

Recall that, in any round
t
,
Alg
{}_{\texttt{lat}}
only observes the latent (“compressed”) trajectories
(\widehat{\phi}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{h}),a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{h},r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{h})
as history for choosing policies. We can therefore conclude that, when
\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{h})=\phi^{\star}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{h})
for all
k\in[K+1],h\in[H]
, the distribution over final policies
\widehat{\pi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{\texttt{lat}}
chosen by
Alg
{}_{\texttt{lat}}
will be identical as if we had chosen
\phi^{\star}
as our decoder. In particular, this implies

\displaystyle\En_{\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[\mathbb{I}\left\{G_{t}\right\}\texttt{Risk}(K,\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}},\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}})\right]

\displaystyle=\En_{\phi^{\star}}\left[\mathbb{I}\left\{G_{t}\right\}\texttt{Risk}(K,\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}},\phi^{\star},M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\texttt{Risk}_{\star}(K),

(57)

where the second line simply follows by removing the indicator function, recalling that
\texttt{Risk}_{\star}(K)=\En\left[\texttt{Risk}(K,\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}},M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}})\right]
, and using that
\texttt{Risk}(K,\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}},\phi^{\star},M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}})=\texttt{Risk}(K,\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}},M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}})
.

Then, we have:

\displaystyle\En\left[\texttt{Risk}_{\texttt{obs}}(TK)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\En_{\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}\sim\rho^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[\En_{\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[\texttt{Risk}(K,\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}},\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}})\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\En_{\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}\sim\rho^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[\En_{\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[\mathbb{I}\left\{G_{t}\right\}\texttt{Risk}(K,\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}},\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}})\right]\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\En_{\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}\sim\rho^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[\En_{\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[\mathbb{I}\left\{\neg G_{t}\right\}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\texttt{Risk}_{\star}(K)+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{P}(\neg G_{t})

\displaystyle=\texttt{Risk}_{\star}(K)+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{P}(\neg G_{t}),

where the first equality applies the tower rule for conditional expectation, the second equality applies linearity of conditional expectations and the upper bound
\texttt{Risk}(K,\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}},\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}})\leq 1
, and the third lines applies the upper bound
Eq. (
57
)
.It remains to bound the last term. Here, note that
by a union bound,

\mathbb{P}(\neg G_{t})\leq\En\left[\sum_{k=1}^{K+1}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En_{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}}\mathbb{I}\left\{\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{h})\neq\phi^{\star}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{h})\right\}\right],

where we have used that trajectory
k
in round
t
is sampled from policy
\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}
, which is in turn sampled from
p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}
. Summing over
t
and using the bound in
Eq. (
56
)
concludes the proof.

∎

G.1
Pseudocode and Proofs for
SelfPredict.Opt
(
Lemma

4.2
)

The pseudocode for our self-predictive estimation procedure is given in
Algorithm

4
.

1:

input
: Decoder set
\Phi
, Latent model class
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
, Mismatch-complete class
\mathcal{L}_{\texttt{lat}}
, Optimism parameter
\gamma

2:

Set
\beta\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\nicefrac{{C_{\texttt{cov}}H\log(T)}}{{T}}}

3:

for

t=1,2,\cdots,T

do

4:

Get dataset
\mathcal{D}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}=\{x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1}\}_{i\in[t-1],h\in[H]}

5:

Compute

\displaystyle\widehat{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}\circ\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}=\argmax_{[M\circ\phi]\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\Phi}\Bigg\{

\displaystyle(\gamma\beta)^{-1}J_{M}(\pi_{M})+\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log\left(\left[M_{h}\circ\phi_{h}\right](r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},\phi_{h+1}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})\mid x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})\right)

(58)

\displaystyle\quad-\max_{[M^{\prime}\circ\phi^{\prime}]\in\mathcal{L}_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\Phi}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log\left(\left[M^{\prime}_{h}\circ\phi^{\prime}_{h}\right](r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},\phi_{h+1}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})\mid x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})\right)\Bigg\}.

(59)

6:

Return
\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}=\left\{\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}\right\}_{h\in[H]}
.

7:

end

for

Algorithm 4

Optimistic Self-Predictive Latent Model Estimation (
SelfPredict.Opt
)

Our main result concerning the
SelfPredict.Opt
estimator for online optimistic self-predictive estimation is the following. We recall our notation for the instantaneous self-prediction error

\left[\Delta_{h}(M_{\texttt{lat}},\phi)\right](x_{h},a_{h})\coloneqq D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M_{\scriptscriptstyle{\texttt{lat},h}}(\phi_{\scriptscriptstyle{h}}(x_{h}),a_{h}),\big[\phi_{\scriptscriptstyle{h+1}}\sharp M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs},h}\big](x_{h},a_{h})\right).

See
4.2

Proof of
Lemma

4.2
.

We will firstly establish that the algorithm obtains low
offline
estimation error.

Lemma G.1

(
SelfPredict.Opt
attains low offline estimation error)
.

For any
\gamma>0
, under decoder realizability (
\phi^{\star}\in\Phi
), model realizability (
M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
), and mismatch function completeness with class
\mathcal{L}_{\texttt{lat}}
(
Assumption

4.4
), the estimator in
Algorithm

4
with inputs
\Phi
,
M_{\texttt{lat}}
,
\mathcal{L}_{\texttt{lat}}
, and
\gamma
satisfies that for all
t\in[T]
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sum_{h=0}^{H}\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}

\displaystyle\En_{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}\left[[\Delta_{h}(\widehat{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}})](x_{h},a_{h})\right]+\gamma^{-1}\left(J^{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}}(\pi_{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}})-J^{\widehat{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}(\pi_{\widehat{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\log(\abs{\cMlat}\abs{\cL_\lat}\abs{\Phi}HT\deltainv)\right).

(60)

Given this result, we can appeal to offline-to-online conversions to establish the final result. Let
C_{\texttt{cov}}\coloneqq C_{\texttt{cov}}(M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}},\Pi_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\Phi)
denote the (state-action) coverability coefficient in
M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}
over the set of policies
\Pi_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\Phi
. Note that by
Lemma

C.1
we have
C_{\texttt{cov},\texttt{st}}(M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}},\Pi_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\Phi)=C_{\texttt{cov},\texttt{st}}
and therefore by
Lemma

C.4
we have
C_{\texttt{cov}}(M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}},\Pi_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\Phi)\leq C_{\texttt{cov},\texttt{st}}\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert
. Let
\eta>0
be a parameter to be chosen later, and
\beta_{\texttt{off}}=\mathcal{O}\left(\log(\abs{\cMlat}\abs{\cL_\lat}\abs{\Phi}HT\deltainv)\right)
be the offline estimation error guaranteed by
Lemma

G.1
. We abbreviate
\alpha\coloneqq\sqrt{C_{\texttt{cov}}H\log(T)}
,
\En^{p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}[\cdot]\coloneqq\En_{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}[\cdot]
, and
\En^{\widetilde{p}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\En_{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}[\cdot]
. Then, we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En^{p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[[\Delta_{h}(\widehat{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}})](x_{h},a_{h})\right]+\gamma^{-1}\left(J^{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}}(\pi_{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}})-J^{\widehat{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}(\pi_{\widehat{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\sqrt{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En^{\widetilde{p}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[[\Delta_{h}(\widehat{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}})](x_{h},a_{h})\right]}+\mathcal{O}(HC_{\texttt{cov}})+\gamma^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(J^{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}}(\pi_{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}})-J^{\widehat{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}(\pi_{\widehat{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\left(\frac{\eta}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En^{\widetilde{p}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[[\Delta_{h}(\widehat{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}})](x_{h},a_{h})\right]+\frac{1}{2\eta}\right)+\gamma^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(J^{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}}(\pi_{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}})-J^{\widehat{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}(\pi_{\widehat{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}})\right)+\mathcal{O}(HC_{\texttt{cov}})

where in the first inequality we have used
Lemma

B.7
with
g^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{h}=\Delta_{h}(\widehat{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}})
and in the second inequality we have used the AM-GM inequality with parameter
\eta
. Collecting terms, we proceed via:

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha\eta}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En^{\widetilde{p}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[\Delta_{h}(\widehat{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}})(x_{h},a_{h})\right]+(\frac{\gamma\eta\alpha}{2})^{-1}\left(J^{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}}(\pi_{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}})-J^{\widehat{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}(\pi_{\widehat{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}})\right)\right)+\frac{\alpha}{2\eta}+\mathcal{O}(HC_{\texttt{cov}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha\eta}{2}T\beta_{\texttt{off}}+\frac{\alpha}{2\eta}+\mathcal{O}(HC_{\texttt{cov}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{C_{\texttt{cov},\texttt{st}}\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert H\log(T)T}\beta_{\texttt{off}}+HC_{\texttt{cov},\texttt{st}}\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{HC_{\texttt{cov},\texttt{st}}\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert T\log(T)}\log(\abs{\cMlat}\abs{\cL_\lat}\abs{\Phi}HT\deltainv)\right),

where in the first inequality we have used
Lemma

G.1
and the definition of
\gamma
in
Algorithm

4
(cf.
Eq. (
58
)
) and in the second inequality we have chosen
\eta=\nicefrac{{1}}{{\sqrt{T}}}
to balance the terms and used the bound
C_{\texttt{cov}}\leq C_{\texttt{cov},\texttt{st}}\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert
.
We convert to an expected regret bound by picking
\delta
appropriately, which gives the final result. It remains to show
Lemma

G.1
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

G.1
.

Fix an iteration
t\in[T]
, and abbreviate
\widehat{M}\coloneqq\widehat{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}
and
\widehat{\phi}\coloneqq\widehat{\phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}
. We follow the analysis of maximum likelihood estimation from
[
Gee00
,
Zha06
,
AKKS20
]
. In particular, we quote Lemma 24 of
[
AKKS20
]
, which in an abstract conditional estimation framework with density class
\mathcal{F}
states the following.

Lemma G.2

(Lemma 24 of
[
AKKS20
]
)
.

Let
D=\{(x_{i},y_{i})\}
be a dataset collected with
x_{i}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}(x_{1:i-1},y_{1:i-1})
and
y_{i}\sim f^{\star}(\cdot\mid x_{i})
,
L(f,D)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(f,(x_{i},y_{i}))
be any loss function that decomposes additively,
\widehat{f}:D\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
be an estimator,
D^{\prime}
be a tangent sequence
D^{\prime}=\{(\widetilde{x}_{i},\widetilde{y}_{i})\}
sampled independently via
\widetilde{x}_{i}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}(x_{1:i-1},y_{1:i-1})
and
\widetilde{y}_{i}\sim f^{\star}(\cdot\mid\widetilde{x}_{i})
. Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:decoupling}-\log\En_{D^{\prime}}\exp\left(L(\widehat{f}(D),D^{\prime})\right)\leq-L(\widehat{f}(D),D)+\log(\abs{\cF}\deltainv),

(61)

For our purposes, we have that
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\Phi
, the data distribution is collected adaptively (for each
h\in[H]
) via
\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}
,
x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h}\sim d^{M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}},\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}_{h}
, and
r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1}\sim M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}(\cdot\mid x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})
. For the loss function
L
, we take

\displaystyle L((M,\phi),D)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{h=0}^{H}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\log(\frac{\Mstarobs(r\ind{i}_{h+1},\phi_{h+1}(x\ind{i}_{h+1})\mid x\ind{i}_h,a\ind{i}_h)}{\brk{M_h\circ\phi_h}(r\ind{i}_h,\phi_{h+1}(x\ind{i}_{h+1}) \mid x\ind{i}_h,a\ind{i}_h)})-\frac{\gamma^{-1}}{2}(J^{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}}(\pi_{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}})-J^{M}(\pi_{M})).

We begin by upper bounding the quantity
-L((\widehat{M},\widehat{\phi})(D),D)
appearing on the right-hand side of
Eq. (
61
)
, or equivalently lower bounding
L((\widehat{M},\widehat{\phi})(D),D)
. Let us abbreviate
\widehat{V}=J^{\widehat{M}}(\pi_{\widehat{M}})
and
V^{\star}=J^{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}}(\pi_{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}})
. Towards this, note that

\displaystyle L((\widehat{M},\widehat{\phi})(D),D)

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=0}^{H}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\log\left(\left[\widehat{M}_{h}\circ\widehat{\phi}_{h}\right](r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},\widehat{\phi}_{h+1}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})\mid x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{h=0}^{H}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\log\left(M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}(r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},\widehat{\phi}_{h+1}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})\mid x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})\right)+\frac{\gamma^{-1}}{2}(\widehat{V}-V^{\star})

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{h=0}^{H}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\log\left(\left[\widehat{M}_{h}\circ\widehat{\phi}_{h}\right](r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},\widehat{\phi}_{h+1}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})\mid x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{h=0}^{H}\max_{[M^{\prime}\circ\phi^{\prime}]\in\mathcal{L}_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\Phi}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\log\left(\left[M^{\prime}_{h}\circ\phi^{\prime}_{h}\right](r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},\widehat{\phi}_{h+1}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})\mid x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})\right)+\frac{\gamma^{-1}}{2}(\widehat{V}-V^{\star})

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{h=0}^{H}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\log\left(\left[M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat},h}\circ\phi^{\star}_{h}\right](r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},\phi^{\star}_{h+1}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})\mid x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{h=0}^{H}\max_{[M^{\prime}\circ\phi^{\prime}]\in\mathcal{L}_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\Phi}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\log\left(\left[M^{\prime}_{h}\circ\phi^{\prime}_{h}\right](r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},\phi^{\star}_{h+1}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})\mid x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})\right)+\frac{\gamma^{-1}}{2}(V^{\star}-V^{\star})

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=0}^{H}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\log\left(\left[M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat},h}\circ\phi^{\star}_{h}\right](r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},\phi^{\star}_{h+1}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})\mid x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{h=0}^{H}\max_{[M^{\prime}\circ\phi^{\prime}]\in\mathcal{L}_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\Phi}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\log\left(\left[M^{\prime}_{h}\circ\phi^{\prime}_{h}\right](r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},\phi^{\star}_{h+1}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})\mid x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})\right),

where in the second line we have used
Lemma

C.8
with
Assumption

4.4
and in the third line we have used the ERM property of
\widehat{M}\circ\widehat{\phi}
together with decoder and model realizability. We claim that this implies

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:mle-concentration-lb}L((\widehat{M},\widehat{\phi})(D),D)\geq-\log(\abs{\cL_\lat\circ\Phi}H\deltainv)

(62)

by concentration. Indeed, for each
h\in[H],i\in[t],
and
[M^{\prime}\circ\phi^{\prime}]\in\mathcal{L}_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\Phi
, let

Z^{[M^{\prime}\circ\phi^{\prime}]}_{i,h}=-\frac{1}{2}\log(\frac{\Mstarobs(r\ind{i}_h,\phistar_{h+1}(x\ind{i}_{h+1}) \mid x\ind{i}_h,a\ind{i}_h)}{\brk{M' \circ\phi'}(r\ind{i}_h,\phistar_{h+1}(x\ind{i}_{h+1}) \mid x\ind{i}_h,a\ind{i}_h)})

Applying
Lemma

B.1
, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}\log(\frac{\Mstarobs(r\ind{i}_h,\phistar_{h+1}(x\ind{i}_{h+1}) \mid x\ind{i}_h,a\ind{i}_h)}{\brk{M' \circ\phi'}(r\ind{i}_h,\phistar_{h+1}(x\ind{i}_{h+1}) \mid x\ind{i}_h,a\ind{i}_h)})

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{t}-2\log( \En_{\pi\ind{i}\sim p\ind{i}}\En^{\pi\ind{i}}\brk*{\exp\prn*{-\frac{1}{2}\log\prn*{\frac{\Mstarobs(r\ind{i}_h,\phistar_{h+1}(x\ind{i}_{h+1}) \mid x\ind{i}_h,a\ind{i}_h)}{\brk{M' \circ\phi'}(r\ind{i}_h,\phistar_{h+1}(x\ind{i}_{h+1}) \mid x\ind{i}_h,a\ind{i}_h)}}}})_{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}(r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},\phi^{\star}_{h+1}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})\mid x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})}{[M^{\prime}\circ\phi^{\prime}](r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},\phi^{\star}_{h+1}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})\mid x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})}\right)\right)\right]-\log(\delta^{-1}),

(63)

with probability at least
1-\delta
, where we have recalled that data is gathered adaptively according to
\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}
.We now quote the following lemma from
[
Zha06
,
AKKS20
]
.

Lemma G.3

(Lemma 25 of
[
AKKS20
]
)
.

For any
\mathcal{D}\in\Delta(\mathcal{X})
and
p,q\in[\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{Y})]
, we have

-2\log\En_{x\sim\mathcal{D},y\sim q(\cdot\mid x)}\exp(-\frac{1}{2} \log(\nicefrac{q(y \mid x)}{p(y \mid x)}))\geq\En_{x\sim\mathcal{D}}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(q(\cdot\mid x),p(\cdot\mid x)\right)\right]

Proof of
Lemma

G.3
.

We include the proof for completeness. The result follows via the following steps.

\displaystyle-2\log\En_{x\sim\mathcal{D},y\sim q(\cdot\mid x)}\exp(-\frac{1}{2} \log(\nicefrac{q(y \mid x)}{p(y \mid x)}))

\displaystyle=-2\log\En_{x\sim\mathcal{D},y\sim q(\cdot\mid x)}\sqrt{\nicefrac{{p(y\mid x)}}{{q(y\mid x)}}}

\displaystyle\geq 2\left(1-\En_{x\sim\mathcal{D},y\sim q(\cdot\mid x)}\sqrt{\nicefrac{{p(y\mid x)}}{{q(y\mid x)}}}\right)

(
\forall x:\log(x)\leq x-1
)

\displaystyle=\En_{x\sim\mathcal{D}}\left[2\left(1-\En_{y\sim q(\cdot\mid x)}\sqrt{\nicefrac{{p(y\mid x)}}{{q(y\mid x)}}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\En_{x\sim\mathcal{D}}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(p(\cdot\mid x),q(\cdot\mid x)\right)\right]

∎

By
Lemma

G.3
, we have that the right-hand-side of
Eq. (
63
)
is further lower bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}\log(\frac{\Mstarobs(r\ind{i}_h,\phistar_{h+1}(x\ind{i}_{h+1}) \mid x\ind{i}_h,a\ind{i}_h)}{\brk{M' \circ\phi'}(r\ind{i}_h,\phistar_{h+1}(x\ind{i}_{h+1}) \mid x\ind{i}_h,a\ind{i}_h)})

\displaystyle\quad\geq\sum_{i=1}^{t}\En_{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(\phi^{\star}_{h+1}\sharp M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}(\cdot\mid x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h}),[M^{\prime}\circ\phi^{\prime}](\cdot\mid x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h})\right)\right]-\log(\delta^{-1})

\displaystyle\quad\geq-\log(\delta^{-1}),

where the last line follows from the non-negativity of squared Hellinger. Taking a union bound over
M^{\prime}\circ\phi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\Phi
and
h\in[H]
gives the desired lower bound in
Eq. (
62
)
.

To conclude the proof, it remains to lower bound the left-hand side in
Eq. (
61
)
. Here, note that:

\displaystyle-\log\En_{D^{\prime}}\exp\left(L((\widehat{M},\hat{\phi})(D),D^{\prime})\right)+\frac{\gamma^{-1}}{2}(V^{\star}-\widehat{V})

\displaystyle=-\log\En_{D^{\prime}}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\log(\frac{\Mstarobs(\wt{r}\ind{i}_h,\wh{\phi}_{h+1}(\wt{x}\ind{i}_{h+1})\mid x\ind{i}_h,a\ind{i}_h)}{\brk*{\wh{M}_h \circ\hatphi_h}(r\ind{i}_h,\wh{\phi}_{h+1}(x\ind{i}_{h+1}) \mid x\ind{i}_h,a\ind{i}_h)})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=-\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\log\En_{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\log(\frac{\Mstarobs(r\ind{i}_h,\wh{\phi}_{h+1}(x\ind{i}_{h+1})\mid x\ind{i}_h,a\ind{i}_h)}{\brk*{\wh{M}_h\circ\hatphi_h}(r\ind{i}_h,\wh{\phi}_{h+1}(x\ind{i}_{h+1}) \mid x\ind{i}_h,a\ind{i}_h)})\right)\right],

(64)

where we have used that in the “tangent sequence”
D^{\prime}
the current sample
(\widetilde{r}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},\widetilde{x}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})
is independent of
(r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h},x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h+1})
. To bound this term, we again appeal to
Lemma

G.3
, concluding that

\displaystyle-\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\log\En_{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\log(\frac{\Mstarobs(r\ind{i}_h,\wh{\phi}_{h+1}(x\ind{i}_{h+1})\mid x\ind{i}_h,a\ind{i}_h)}{\brk*{\wh{M}_h \circ\hatphi_h}(r\ind{i}_h,\wh{\phi}_{h+1}(x\ind{i}_{h+1}) \mid x\ind{i}_h,a\ind{i}_h)})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad\geq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\En_{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left([\widehat{M}_{h}\circ\widehat{\phi}_{h}](x_{h},a_{h}),\widehat{\phi}_{h+1}\sharp M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}(x_{h},a_{h})\right)\right]

Combining everything, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\En_{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(\left[\widehat{M}_{h}\circ\widehat{\phi}_{h}\right](x_{h},a_{h}),\widehat{\phi}_{h+1}\sharp M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}(x_{h},a_{h})\right)\right]+\gamma^{-1}(V^{\star}-\widehat{V})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\log(\abs{\cL_\lat}\abs{\Phi}H\deltainv)+\log(\abs{\cMlat}\abs{\Phi}\deltainv)

Taking an additional union bound over
t\in[T]
, we have that with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\En_{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{h}}

\displaystyle\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(\left[\widehat{M}_{h}\circ\widehat{\phi}_{h}\right](x_{h},a_{h}),\widehat{\phi}_{h+1}\sharp M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}(x_{h},a_{h})\right)\right]+\gamma^{-1}\left(J^{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}}(\pi_{M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}})-J^{M^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}(\pi_{M^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\log(\abs{\cMlat}\abs{\cL_\lat}\abs{\Phi}HT\deltainv)\right),

for all
t\in[T]
, as desired.

∎

See
4.2

Proof of
Corollary

4.2
.

The first inequality simply follows by plugging the bound of
\texttt{Est}_{\texttt{self};\texttt{opt}}
from
Lemma

4.2
into
Theorem

4.2
. For the second inequality, let
\Delta=c_{2}\sqrt{HC_{\texttt{cov},\texttt{st}}\lvert\mathcal{A}\rvert}\log(\abs{\cMlat}\abs{\cL_\lat}\abs{\Phi})
. The result follows by setting
\gamma
s.t.
c_{3}\gamma^{-1}HK=\texttt{Risk}_{\texttt{base}}(K)
i.e.
\gamma=c_{3}\frac{KH}{\texttt{Risk}_{\texttt{base}}(K)}
,
and
T
such that
\frac{\gamma K\Delta}{\sqrt{T}}=\texttt{Risk}_{\texttt{base}}(K)
i.e.
T=\frac{K^{4}\Delta^{2}\gamma^{2}}{\texttt{Risk}_{\texttt{base}}(K)^{2}}=\frac{K^{4}\Delta^{2}H^{2}}{\left(\texttt{Risk}_{\texttt{base}}(K)\right)^{4}}
.
Then the result follows by direct substitution and by noting that
\frac{K}{T}\leq 1
since
\texttt{Risk}_{\texttt{base}}(K)\leq 1
.

∎

G.2
Proofs for Main Risk Bound (
Theorem

4.2
)

Our main risk bound (
Theorem

4.2
) follows as a special case of a more general theorem (
Theorem

G.1
), which holds for algorithm that satisfies a property we refer to as
CorruptionRobust
-ness (
Definition

H.2
). We now state the more general theorem, postponing its proof (and a formal definition of corruption robustness) until
Appendix

H
.

Theorem G.1

(Risk bound for
O2L
under self-predictive estimation and
CorruptionRobustness
)
.

Assume
Rep
{}_{\texttt{self};\texttt{opt}}
satisfies
Assumption

4.3
with parameter
\gamma>0
and that
\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
is realizable (i.e.
M^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
).
Furthermore, let
Alg
{}_{\texttt{lat}}
be
CorruptionRobust
(
Definition

H.2
) with parameter
\alpha
. Then,
O2L
(
Algorithm

1
) with inputs
T,K,\Phi,\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}}
, and
Rep
{}_{\texttt{self};\texttt{opt}}
has expected risk

\En\left[\texttt{Risk}_{\texttt{obs}}(TK)\right]\leq c_{1}\cdot\texttt{Risk}_{\texttt{base}}(K)+c_{2}\gamma\cdot\frac{K}{T}\texttt{Est}_{\texttt{self};\texttt{opt}}(T,\gamma)+c_{3}\gamma^{-1}\cdot\left(\alpha^{2}+H\right)

(65)

for absolute constants
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3}>0
.

Our main risk bound (
Theorem

4.2
) follows from the following lemma, which establishes that
any

Alg
{}_{\texttt{lat}}
is
CorruptionRobust
in the sense of
Definition

H.2
for a sufficiently large corruption robustness parameter. Below, for any POMDP
\widetilde{M}
over state-action space
\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
, we write
\widetilde{M}(s_{1:h},a_{1:h})
for the conditional probability over reward
r_{h}
and
s_{h+1}
given
s_{1:h},a_{1:h}
, i.e.
\widetilde{M}_{h}(s_{1:h},a_{1:h})=\widetilde{M}_{h}(r_{h},s_{h+1}=\cdot\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})
.

Lemma G.4
.

Let
M^{\star}
be any reference MDP and
\widetilde{M}
be any POMDP with the same state and action space. Then for any algorithm
Alg
{}_{\texttt{lat}}
, we have

\displaystyle\En^{\widetilde{M},\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}}}\left[\texttt{Risk}_{M^{\star}}(K)\right]

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\En^{M^{\star},\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}}}\left[\texttt{Risk}_{M^{\star}}(K)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+c_{2}\En^{\widetilde{M},\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}}}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En^{\widetilde{M},\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M^{\star}_{h}(s_{h},a_{h}),\widetilde{M}_{h}(s_{1:h},a_{1:h})\right)\right]\right],

where
c_{1},c_{2}>0
are absolute constants. In particular,
Alg
{}_{\texttt{lat}}
is
CorruptionRobust
(
Definition

H.2
) with
\alpha=c_{2}\sqrt{KH}
.

Proof of
Lemma

G.4
.

Let us abbreviate
{\textsc{Alg}}\coloneqq\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}}
. For
i\in[K]
, let
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}
denote the trajectory
(s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{1},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{1},r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{1},\ldots,s^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{H},a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{H},r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}}_{H})
. Let
\mathbb{P}\coloneqq\mathbb{P}^{M^{\star},{\textsc{Alg}}}
denote the law of
\left\{(\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}},\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}})\right\}_{i\in[K]}
under
Alg
in the true MDP
M^{\star}
, and
\mathbb{Q}\coloneqq\mathbb{P}^{\widetilde{M},{\textsc{Alg}}}
denote the law of
\left\{(\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}},\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{(i)}})\right\}_{i\in[K]}
under
Alg
under the POMDP
\widetilde{M}
. Let us write
M^{\star}(\pi)
and
\widetilde{M}(\pi)
for the laws of trajectory
\tau
sampled from policy
\pi
in
M^{\star}
or
\widetilde{M}
respectively. Let
\widehat{\pi}
denote the policy output by the algorithm after
K
rounds of interaction with the environment. By
Lemma

B.5
we have

\En^{\widetilde{M},{\textsc{Alg}}}\left[J^{M^{\star}}(\pi_{M^{\star}})-J^{M^{\star}}(\widehat{\pi})\right]\leq 3\En^{M^{\star},{\textsc{Alg}}}\left[J^{M^{\star}}(\pi_{M^{\star}})-J^{M^{\star}}(\widehat{\pi})\right]+4D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(\mathbb{P}^{M^{\star},{\textsc{Alg}}},\mathbb{P}^{\widetilde{M},{\textsc{Alg}}}\right).

By the subadditivity property for squared Hellinger distance (
Lemma

B.4
) applied to the sequence
\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}},\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{(1)}},\ldots,\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(K)}},\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{(K)}}
, we have

\displaystyle D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(\mathbb{P}^{M^{\star},{\textsc{Alg}}},\mathbb{P}^{\widetilde{M},{\textsc{Alg}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 7\En^{\widetilde{M},{\textsc{Alg}}}\Bigg[\sum_{k=1}^{K}D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(\mathbb{P}(\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}\mid\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(1:k-1)}},\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{(1:k-1)}}),\mathbb{Q}(\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}\mid\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(1:k-1)}},\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{(1:k-1)}})\right)+

\displaystyle\hskip 75.0001ptD^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(\mathbb{P}(\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}\mid\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(1:k)}},\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{(1:k-1)}}),\mathbb{Q}(\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}\mid\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(1:k)}},\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{(1:k-1)}})\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=7\En^{\widetilde{M},{\textsc{Alg}}}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{K}D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(\mathbb{P}(\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}\mid\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(1:k)}},\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{(1:k-1)}}),\mathbb{Q}(\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}\mid\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(1:k)}},\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{(1:k-1)}})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=7\En^{\widetilde{M},{\textsc{Alg}}}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{K}D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M^{\star}(\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}),\widetilde{M}(\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 49\En^{\widetilde{M},{\textsc{Alg}}}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En^{\widetilde{M},\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M^{\star}_{h}(s_{h},a_{h}),\widetilde{M}_{h}(s_{1:h},a_{1:h})\right)\right]\right]

where in the second step we have used that
\mathbb{P}(\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}\mid\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(1:k)}},\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{(1:k-1)}})=\mathbb{Q}(\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}\mid\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(1:k)}},\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{(1:k-1)}})
since the histories are equivalent, in the third step we have used that the trajectories are generated by the MDP/PODMP
M^{\star}
and
\widetilde{M}
, respectively, in the fourth step we have again applied the subadditivity property of the squared Hellinger distance (
Lemma

B.4
) to the sequence
(s_{1},a_{1},r_{1},\ldots,s_{H},a_{H},r_{H})
.∎

See
4.2

Proof of
Theorem

4.2
.

This follows from
Theorem

G.1
as well as
Lemma

G.4
, by taking
\alpha=c_{2}\sqrt{KH}
and simplifying. ∎

Appendix H
Additional Results for
Section

4.3
: Self-Predictive Estimation

This section contains a more general result for algorithmic modularity
under self-predictive estimation (
Theorem

G.1
),
from which our main result is derived as a special case, along with
associated background, applications, and proofs. This section is organized as follows.

•

Section

H.1
presents: definitions for the

\phi
-compressed POMDP and
CorruptionRobust
algorithms
(
Section

H.1.1
), statements for properties of the

\phi
-compressed dynamics (
Section

H.1.2
). The
risk bound for
O2L
under self-predictive estimation and

CorruptionRobustness
(
Theorem

G.1
) is given in
Section

H.1.3
, and a statement that the
Golf
algorithm is
CorruptionRobust
(
Section

H.1.4
).

•

Section

H.2
presents for the proofs for the properties of the
\phi
-compressed POMDPs.

•

Section

H.3
presents a proof for the risk bound of
O2L
under self-predictive estimation and
CorruptionRobustness
.

•

Section

H.4
presents a proof that the
Golf
algorithm is
CorruptionRobust
.

H.1
O2L
with Self-predictive Estimation and
CorruptionRobust
Base Algorithms

H.1.1
Definitions:
\phi
-compressed POMDP and
CorruptionRobustness

Consider iteration
k\in[K]
of epoch
t\in[T]
within
O2L
. Suppose that

RepLearn
has chosen decoder
\phi=\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}
. Then,
the latent algorithm has observed the data
\mathcal{D}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}=\{\phi(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{h}),a^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{h},r^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{h},\phi(x^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}_{h+1})\}
collected from the
preceding policies in the epoch:
\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,1)}}_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},\ldots,\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k-1)}}_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}
(
8
). Due to possible inaccuracies in the decoder
\phi
, the dataset
\mathcal{D}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t,k)}}
may not be generated from a Markovian process and must instead be viewed as being generated from a PODMP, formally defined as follows.

Definition H.1

(
\phi
-compressed POMDP)
.

The
\phi
-compressed POMDP
\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi}
induced by
M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}
and
\phi
is defined by:

1.

Latent
state space
\mathcal{X}

2.

Action space
\mathcal{A}

3.

Observation
state space
\mathcal{S}

4.

Latent reward functions
R^{\star}_{\texttt{obs},h}:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{A}\rightarrow[0,1]

5.

Latent dynamics
P^{\star}_{\texttt{obs},h}:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{X})

6.

(Deterministic) observation function
\mathcal{O}_{h}:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}
defined by
\mathcal{O}_{h}(x)=\phi_{h}(x)
,

7.

Horizon
H

8.

Initial latent distribution
P^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}(x_{0}\mid\emptyset)

Note that the latent space
for the POMDP
is the observation
space of the latent-dynamics MDP
M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}
, and vice-versa; we adopt
this terminology because—from the perspective of the base algorithm,
the observations
x_{h}
can be viewed as a Markovian (yet partially
observed process) that generates the learned states
\phi(x_{h})
on
which the algorithm acts.
We write
\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}\coloneqq\mathbb{P}^{\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi},{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}
for the probability distribution over trajectories
(x_{h},s_{h},a_{h},r_{h})_{h\in[H]}
in the
\phi
-compressed POMDP when playing policy
{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}:\mathcal{S}\times[H]\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{A})
, where
x_{h}\in\mathcal{X}
are the POMDP’s latent states,
s_{h}\in\mathcal{S}
are the observed states, and
a_{h}\in\mathcal{A}
are the actions. We let
\widetilde{\En}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}\coloneqq\En^{\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi},{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}
denote the corresponding expectation. We write
\widetilde{P}_{\phi,h}(s_{h+1}\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})=\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(s_{h+1}\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})
and
\tilde{r}_{\phi,h}(r_{h}\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})=\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(r_{h}\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})
for the conditional distributions of next states and rewards given the first
h
state-action pairs, which are policy-independent. We also write
\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi}(r_{h},s_{h+1}\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})=\tilde{r}_{\phi,h}(r_{h}\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})\widetilde{P}_{\phi,h}(s_{h+1}\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})
for the joint one-step probability. We will abbreviate
\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi}(s_{1:h},a_{1:h})\coloneqq\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi}(r_{h},s_{h+1}=\cdot\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})
.

Note that for any
{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}
,
\widetilde{P}^{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}_{\phi,h}(s_{h+1}\mid s_{h},a_{h})
is a well-defined (Markovian, policy-dependent) probability kernel, which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}^{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}_{\phi,h}(s_{h+1}\mid s_{h},a_{h})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s_{1:h-1},a_{1:h-1}}\widetilde{P}^{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}_{\phi,h}(s_{1:h-1},a_{1:h-1}\mid s_{h},a_{h})\widetilde{P}_{\phi,h}(s_{h+1}\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})

(66)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\En}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}\left[\widetilde{P}_{\phi,h}(s_{h+1}\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})\mid s_{h},a_{h}\right]

(67)

Similarly,
\tilde{r}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi,h}(r_{h}\mid s_{h},a_{h})
is a Markovian and policy-dependent reward distribution which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\tilde{r}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi,h}(r_{h}\mid s_{h},a_{h})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s_{1:h-1},a_{1:h-1}}\widetilde{P}^{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}_{\phi,h}(s_{1:h-1},a_{1:h-1}\mid s_{h},a_{h})\tilde{r}_{\phi,h}(r_{h}\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})

(68)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\En}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}\left[\tilde{r}_{\phi,h}(r_{h}\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})\mid s_{h},a_{h}\right].

(69)

Finally, we let

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:mphi-pomdp-definition}\widetilde{M}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}},\star}_{\phi,h}(r_{h},s_{h+1}\mid s_{h},a_{h})=\widetilde{\En}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}\left[\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi}(r_{h},s_{h+1}\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})\mid s_{h},a_{h}\right]

(70)

denote the associated one-step model over joint rewards and transitions.

Our
CorruptionRobustness
condition asserts that the agent—when
observing data from the
\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}
-compressed dynamics

\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi}
—attains a risk bound for
M_{\texttt{lat}}
which is proportional to
its risk when observing data from
M_{\texttt{lat}}
itself, plus a term that
captures the degree of misspecification between
\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi}
and
M_{\texttt{lat}}
.

Definition H.2

(
CorruptionRobust
algorithm)
.

We say that
Alg
{}_{\texttt{lat}}
is
CorruptionRobust
with parameters
\alpha
and
\texttt{Risk}_{\texttt{base}}
if there exists a constant
c_{1}
such that, for any
(\phi,M_{\texttt{lat}})\in\Phi\times\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}
, we have

\displaystyle\En^{\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi},\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}}}\left[\texttt{Risk}(K,\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}},M_{\texttt{lat}})\right]

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\cdot\texttt{Risk}_{\texttt{base}}(K)

\displaystyle\hskip-30.00005pt+\alpha\En^{\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi},\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}}}\left[\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En_{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}_{\texttt{lat}}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}}\widetilde{\En}_{\phi}^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}_{\texttt{lat}}}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M_{\texttt{lat},h}(s_{h},a_{h}),\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi,h}(s_{1:h},a_{1:h})\right)\right]}\right],

where we recall the definition of the random variable
\texttt{Risk}(K,\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}},M_{\texttt{lat}})
from
Eq. (
1
)
,
the expectation
\En^{\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi},\textnormal{{Alg${}_{\texttt{lat}}$}}}
denotes the interaction protocol of
Alg
{}_{\texttt{lat}}
in the
\phi
-compressed dynamics
\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi}
, and
p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}
denotes the randomization distribution over latent policies that
Alg
{}_{\texttt{lat}}
plays.

H.1.2
Basic properties of the
\phi
-compressed dynamics (
Definition

H.1
)

We establish a number of basic properties for the
\phi
-compressed POMDP and their relation to the self-prediction guarantee obtained by
Rep
{}_{\texttt{self};\texttt{opt}}
. These properties are proved in
Section

H.2
. Firstly, we have the following change-of-measure lemma:

Lemma H.1

(Change of measure lemma)
.

For any
\phi\in\Phi
,
f\in[\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\rightarrow[0,1]]
,
h\in[H]
, and
{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\in[\mathcal{S}\times[H]\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{A})]
, we have:

\widetilde{\En}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}\left[f(s_{h},a_{h})\right]=\En^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}\left[[f\circ\phi](x_{h},a_{h})\right].\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:com}

(71)

The next lemma states that the kernels of the
\phi
-compressed POMDP are well-approximated by the (Markovian) latent model fit by
Rep
{}_{\texttt{self};\texttt{opt}}
. We recall the instantaneous self-prediction error

\left[\Delta_{h}(M_{\texttt{lat}},\phi)\right](x_{h},a_{h})\coloneqq D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M_{\scriptscriptstyle{\texttt{lat},h}}(\phi_{\scriptscriptstyle{h}}(x_{h}),a_{h}),\big[\phi_{\scriptscriptstyle{h+1}}\sharp M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs},h}\big](x_{h},a_{h})\right).

Lemma H.2

(Near-markovianity of the
\phi
-compressed dynamics)
.

For any decoder
\phi
, base model
M_{\texttt{lat}}
, and policy
\pi_{\texttt{lat}}:\mathcal{S}\times[H]\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{A})
, we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{h=0}^{H}\widetilde{\En}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M_{\texttt{lat},h}(s_{h},a_{h}),\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi,h}(s_{1:h},a_{1:h})\right)\right]\leq\sum_{h=0}^{H}\En^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}\left[[\Delta_{h}(M_{\texttt{lat}},\phi)](x_{h},a_{h})\right].

(72)

Furthermore, we also have

\displaystyle\sum_{h=0}^{H}\widetilde{\En}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M_{\texttt{lat},h}(s_{h},a_{h}),\widetilde{M}^{\star,{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi,h}(s_{h},a_{h})\right)\right]\leq\sum_{h=0}^{H}\En^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}\left[[\Delta_{h}(M_{\texttt{lat}},\phi)](x_{h},a_{h})\right].

(73)

A corollary is the following lemma establishing errors between expectations under
M_{\texttt{lat}}
, the model estimated by
Rep
{}_{\texttt{self};\texttt{opt}}
, and those under the
\phi
-compressed POMDP
\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi}
.

Lemma H.3

(Simulation lemma)
.

For any latent model
M_{\texttt{lat}}
with Markovian transition kernel
\{P_{\texttt{lat},h}\}_{h\in[H]}
, latent policy
{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}:\mathcal{S}\times[H]\rightarrow\Delta(\mathcal{A})
, and decoder
\phi\in\Phi
, we have that for all
f:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\rightarrow[0,1]
:

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:fourtet}\lvert\En^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}[f(s_{h},a_{h})]-\widetilde{\En}^{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}_{\phi}[f(s_{h},a_{h})]\rvert\leq\sum_{h^{\prime}<h}\En^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}\left[\left\|\left[P_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\phi\right]_{h}(x_{h^{\prime}},a_{h^{\prime}})-\phi_{h+1}\sharp P^{\star}_{\texttt{obs},h}(x_{h^{\prime}},a_{h^{\prime}})\right\|_{\texttt{tv}}\right],

(74)

and thus for any sequence of policies
\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{\texttt{lat}}
, latent models
M_{\texttt{lat}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}
, and decoders
\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}
, we have:

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=0}^{H}\lvert\En^{M_{\texttt{lat}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{\texttt{lat}}}[f(s_{h},a_{h})]-\widetilde{\En}^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{\texttt{lat}}}_{\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}[f(s_{h},a_{h})]\rvert\leq H\sqrt{TH}\sqrt{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{h=0}^{H}\En^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{\texttt{lat}}\circ\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\left[[\Delta_{h}(M^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}_{\texttt{lat}},\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}})](x_{h},a_{h})\right]}.

H.1.3
Risk bound for
O2L
under
CorruptionRobustness

We state the main risk bound for
O2L
under self-predictive estimation and the above definition of corruption robustness.

See
G.1

H.1.4
Examples of
CorruptionRobust
algorithms

In this section, we establish that the
Golf
algorithm satisfies the

CorruptionRobust
definition (
Definition

H.2
) with a parameter

\alpha\approx K^{-1/2}
. This improves upon the rate that would be
obtained by invoking the generic guarantee in

Lemma

G.4
. We expect that several other
algorithms can be analyzed in a similar way, thereby leading to tight
rates in the same fashion. We restate the pseudocode in
Algorithm

5
for convenience.

Algorithm 5

GOLF

[
JLM21
]

input:
Function classes
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
, confidence width
\beta>0
.

initialize:

\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(0)}}\leftarrow\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{D}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(0)}}\leftarrow\emptyset\;\;\forall h\in[H]
.

1:

for
episode
t=1,2,\dotsc,T

do

2:

Select policy
\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}\leftarrow\pi_{f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}
, where
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}\vcentcolon={}\argmax_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t-1)}}}f(x_{1},\pi_{f,1}(x_{1}))
.

3:

Execute
\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}
for one episode and obtain trajectory
(x_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},a_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},r_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}),\ldots,(x_{H}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},a_{H}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},r_{H}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}})
.

4:

Update dataset:
\mathcal{D}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}\leftarrow\mathcal{D}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t-1)}}\cup\big\{\big(x_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},a_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}},x_{h+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}\big)\big\}\;\;\forall h\in[H]
.

5:

Compute confidence set:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}\leftarrow\bigg\{f\in\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{L}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}(f_{h},f_{h+1})-\min_{g_{h}\in\mathcal{G}_{h}}\mathcal{L}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}(g_{h},f_{h+1})\leq\beta\;\;\forall h\in[H]\bigg\},

\displaystyle\text{where \quad}\mathcal{L}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}(f,f^{\prime})\coloneqq\sum_{(x,a,r,x^{\prime})\in\mathcal{D}_{h}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(t)}}}\Big(f(x,a)-r-\max_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}f^{\prime}(x^{\prime},a^{\prime})\Big)^{2},\penalty\ \forall f,f^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}.

6:

end

for

7:

Output
\widehat{\pi}=\texttt{Unif}(\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(1:T)}})
.

Let
M_{\texttt{lat}}=(r_{\texttt{lat}},P_{\texttt{lat}})
be given, and we let
Q^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}\coloneqq Q^{M_{\texttt{lat}},\star}
, and
\mathcal{T}_{\texttt{lat},h}f(s,a)\coloneqq r_{\texttt{lat},h}(s,a)+\En_{s^{\prime}\sim P_{\texttt{lat},h}(s,a)}\left[V_{f}(s^{\prime})\right]
. We assume that the algorithm has a latent function class
\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{alg}}
which realizes
Q^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}
, as well as a helper class
\mathcal{G}_{\texttt{alg}}
which is
\mathcal{T}_{\texttt{lat}}
-complete for
\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{alg}}
.

Assumption H.1

(
\mathcal{T}_{\texttt{lat}}
-completeness)
.

We have:

Q^{\star}_{\texttt{lat}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{alg}},\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{T}_{\texttt{lat}}\mathcal{F}_{\texttt{alg}}\subseteq\mathcal{G}_{\texttt{alg}}.

For our analysis of
Golf
, it is most natural to quantify the corruption levels in the following way.

Assumption H.2

(Corruption levels of
M_{\texttt{lat}}
and
\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi}
)
.

Let
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{rep}}^{2}
be such that, for any sequence of policies

\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}_{\texttt{lat}}
played by the algorithm when interacting
with the
\phi
-compressed POMDP, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\En_{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}_{\texttt{lat}}\sim p^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}_{\texttt{lat}}}\widetilde{\En}^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}_{\texttt{lat}}}_{\phi}\left[(r_{\texttt{lat},h}(s_{h},a_{h})-\tilde{r}^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}}_{\phi,h}(s_{h},a_{h}))^{2}+\norm{\Plath(s_h,a_h) - \Pphipikh(s_{h}, a_{h})}^{2}_{\texttt{tv}}\right]\leq\varepsilon_{\mathsf{rep}}^{2}.

(75)

We note that

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mathsf{rep}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\widetilde{\En}^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}_{\texttt{lat}}}_{\phi}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M_{\texttt{lat},h}(s_{h},a_{h}),\widetilde{M}_{\phi,h}^{\star,\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}_{\texttt{lat}}}(s_{h},a_{h})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\widetilde{\En}^{\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}}_{\texttt{lat}}}_{\phi}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M_{\texttt{lat},h}(s_{h},a_{h}),\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi,h}(s_{1:h},a_{1:h})\right)\right]

by the data-processing inequality (cf.
Eq. (
87
)
and
Eq. (
77
)
) and the inequality
\|p-q\|^{2}_{\texttt{tv}}\leq D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(p,q\right)
, and thus a
CorruptionRobustness
bound in terms of
\varepsilon_{\mathsf{rep}}
implies a
CorruptionRobustness
bound in the sense of
Definition

H.2
.

Theorem H.1

(Latent GOLF is
CorruptionRobust
)
.

Under
Assumption

H.1
and
Assumption

H.2
,
Algorithm

5
with
\beta=c\left(\log\left(\lvert\mathcal{F}\rvert\lvert\mathcal{G}\rvert KH\delta^{-1}\right)+\varepsilon_{\mathsf{rep}}\right)
, has regret

\sum_{k=1}^{K}J^{M_{\texttt{lat}}}(\pi_{M_{\texttt{lat}}})-J^{M_{\texttt{lat}}}(\pi^{\scriptscriptstyle{(k)}})\leq\mathcal{O}\left(H\sqrt{C_{\texttt{cov}}K\log(K)\log(\nicefrac{\abs{\cF}\abs{\cG}HK}{\delta})}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(H^{3/2}\sqrt{KC_{\texttt{cov}}\log(K)\varepsilon_{\mathsf{rep}}^{2}}\right),

and consequently is
CorruptionRobust
(
Definition

H.2
) with parameters

\alpha=\frac{H^{3/2}}{\sqrt{K}}\sqrt{C_{\texttt{cov}}\log(K)}\text{ and }\texttt{Risk}_{\texttt{base}}(K)=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{H}{\sqrt{K}}\sqrt{C_{\texttt{cov}}\log(K)\log(\abs{\cF}\abs{\cG}HK)}\right).

Corollary H.1

(
Golf
applied in
O2L
)
.

Let us suppose that the appropriate assumptions for the estimator in
Algorithm

4
to have regret bounded by
\texttt{Est}_{\texttt{self}}(T,\gamma)=\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{HC_{\texttt{cov}}T}\log(\Ccov\abs{\cMlat}\abs{\cL_\lat}\abs{\Phi}HT)\right)
(
Lemma

4.2
) hold. Then, we can take
\gamma\approx K^{-1/2}
and
T\approx K^{4}
, and the bound
Theorem

G.1
gives
an expected risk of
\varepsilon
with a number of trajectories
TK=\poly(C_{\texttt{cov}},H,\log\lvert\mathcal{M}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert,\log\lvert\Phi\rvert,\log\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\texttt{lat}}\rvert)\cdot\nicefrac{{1}}{{\varepsilon^{10}}}
, improving over the

1/\varepsilon^{14}
rate of the universal result
(
Corollary

4.2
).

H.2
Proofs for
Section

H.1.2
: Properties of
\phi
-compressed POMDPs

See
H.1

Proof of
Lemma

H.1
.

Recall that
\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}
denotes the law of
(x_{h},s_{h},a_{h})_{h\in[H]}
in the
\phi
-compressed POMDP when playing policy

{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}
. For clarity, and to differentiate a random variable from its
realization, in the proofs below we will use upper-case notation such
as
\left\{S_{h}=s_{h},A_{h}=a_{h},X_{h}=x_{h}\right\}
to indicate realizations

of random variables in the POMDP.

Let
\tilde{d}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{h}(s,a)=\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(S_{h}=s,A_{h}=a)
be the marginalized occupancy measure for in the
\phi
-compressed POMDP
\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi}
. We write
d^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}_{h}\coloneqq d^{M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}},{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}_{h}
.
The left-hand side in
Eq. (
71
)
is equal to:

\widetilde{\En}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}[f(s_{h},a_{h})]=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S},a\in\mathcal{A}}\tilde{d}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{h}(s,a)f(s,a),

Meanwhile, the right-hand side is equal to:

\En^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}_{h}[[f\circ\phi](x_{h},a_{h})]=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S},a\in\mathcal{A}}f(s,a)\sum_{x:\phi(x)=s}d^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}_{h}(x,a).

So it only remains to show that, for each
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
a\in\mathcal{A}
,
we have
\tilde{d}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{h}(s,a)=\sum_{x:\phi(x)=s}d^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}_{h}(x,a)
. Firstly, note that it is enough to show that

\sum_{x_{h}:\phi(x_{h})=s_{h}}d^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}_{h}(x_{h})=\tilde{d}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{h}(s_{h})
, since
\tilde{d}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{h}(s_{h},a_{h})=\tilde{d}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{h}(s_{h}){\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}(a_{h}\mid s_{h})
and
\sum_{x_{h}:\phi(x_{h})=s_{h}}d^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}_{h}(x_{h},a_{h})=\sum_{x_{h}:\phi(x_{h})=s_{h}}d^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}_{h}(x_{h}){\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}(a_{h}\mid\phi(x_{h}))={\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}(a_{h}\mid s_{h})\sum_{x_{h}:\phi(x_{h})=s_{h}}d^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}_{h}(x_{h})
.
Toward this, we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{x_{h}:\phi(x_{h})=s_{h}}d^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}_{h}(x_{h})

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{h}:\phi(x_{h})=s_{h}}\left(\sum_{x_{h-1},a_{h-1}\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{A}}d_{h-1}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}(x_{h-1},a_{h-1})P^{\star}_{\texttt{obs},h}(x_{h}\mid x_{h-1},a_{h-1})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{h-1},a_{h-1}\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{A}}d_{h-1}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}(x_{h-1},a_{h-1})\sum_{x_{h}:\phi(x_{h})=s_{h}}P^{\star}_{\texttt{obs},h}(x_{h}\mid x_{h-1},a_{h-1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{h-1},a_{h-1}\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{A}}d_{h-1}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}(x_{h-1},a_{h-1})P^{\star}_{\texttt{obs},h}(\phi(x_{h})=s_{h}\mid x_{h-1},a_{h-1})

At the same time,

\displaystyle\tilde{d}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{h}(s_{h})=\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(S_{h}=s_{h})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\widetilde{x},\widetilde{a}}\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(X_{h-1}=\widetilde{x},A_{h-1}=\widetilde{a})\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(S_{h}=s_{h}\mid X_{h-1}=\widetilde{x},A_{h-1}=\widetilde{a})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\widetilde{x},\widetilde{a}}\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(X_{h-1}=\widetilde{x},A_{h-1}=\widetilde{a})P^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}(\phi(x_{h})=s_{h}\mid x_{h-1},a_{h-1}),

where in the second equality we have used the definition of the observation function
s_{h}=\mathcal{O}(x_{h})=\phi(x_{h})
.

To conclude, it remains to show that for all
h
, we have:

d_{h}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}(x_{h},a_{h})=\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(X_{h}=x_{h},A_{h}=a_{h}).

We do this by induction. Again, note that it is sufficient to establish
d_{h}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}(x_{h})=\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(X_{h}=x_{h})
. The case
h=1
is clear. For the general case, we have:

\displaystyle d_{h}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}(x_{h})

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{h-1},a_{h-1}\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{A}}d_{h-1}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}(x_{h-1},a_{h-1})P^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}(x_{h}\mid x_{h-1},a_{h-1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{h-1},a_{h-1}\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{A}}\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(X_{h}=x_{h-1},A_{h-1}=a_{h-1})P^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}(x_{h}\mid x_{h-1},a_{h-1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{h-1},a_{h-1}\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{A}}\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(X_{h}=x_{h-1},A_{h-1}=a_{h-1})\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(X_{h}=x_{h}\mid X_{h-1}=x_{h-1},A_{h-1}=a_{h-1})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(X_{h}=x_{h}).

∎

See
H.2

Proof of
Lemma

H.2
.

We begin with the first event. Note that, for any
{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}
, the PODMP kernel
\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi,h}(r_{h},s_{h+1}=\cdot\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})
can be written as:

\displaystyle\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi,h}(r_{h},s_{h+1}=\cdot\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{h},a_{h}\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{A}}\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(r_{h},s_{h+1}=\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h},s_{1:h},a_{1:h})\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(x_{h},a_{h}\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{h},a_{h}\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{A}}\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(r_{h},s_{h+1}=\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h})\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(x_{h},a_{h}\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h}),

where we have used
\widetilde{M}(r_{h},s_{h+1}=\cdot\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})=\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(r_{h},s_{h+1}=\cdot\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})
, the law of total probability, and that
x_{h},a_{h}
is a sufficient statistic for
r_{h}
and
s_{h+1}
. We further note that

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:justice}\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(r_{h},s_{h+1}=\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h})=M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs},h}(r_{h},\phi_{h+1}(x_{h+1})=\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h}),

(76)

since
s_{h+1}=\mathcal{O}_{h+1}(x_{h+1})=\phi_{h+1}(x_{h+1})
is a deterministic function of
x_{h+1}
and
r_{h},x_{h+1}\sim M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs},h}(x_{h},a_{h})
.
Thus, for a fixed
h
and
t
, and omitting the
h
indices on the decoder
\phi
for cleanliness, the expectation in equation
Eq. (
72
)
becomes:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\En}_{\phi}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M_{\texttt{lat},h}(s_{h},a_{h}),\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi,h}(r_{h},s_{h+1}=\cdot\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s_{1:h},a_{1:h}\in(\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A})^{h}}\widetilde{P}_{\phi}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}(s_{1:h},a_{1:h})\sum_{x_{h},a_{h}}\widetilde{P}_{\phi}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}(x_{h},a_{h}\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M_{\texttt{lat},h}(s_{h},a_{h}),\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(r_{h},s_{h+1}=\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h})\right)

(Jensen)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{1:h},a_{1:h}\in(\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A})^{h}\\
x_{h},a_{h}\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{A}\end{subarray}}\widetilde{P}_{\phi}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}(s_{1:h},a_{1:h})\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(x_{h},a_{h}\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M_{\texttt{lat},h}(\phi(x_{h}),a_{h}),\widetilde{P}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}(r_{h},\phi(x_{h+1})=\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{h},a_{h}\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{A}}\widetilde{P}_{\phi}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}(x_{h},a_{h})D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M_{\texttt{lat}}(\phi(x_{h}),a_{h}),M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs},h}(r_{h},\phi(x_{h+1})=\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h})\right)

(Simplifying &
Eq. (
76
)
)

\displaystyle=\En^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M_{\texttt{lat}}(\phi(x_{h}),a_{h}),M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs},h}(r_{h},\phi(x_{h+1})=\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h})\right)\right]

(Change of measure (
Lemma

H.1
))

\displaystyle=\En^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}\circ\phi}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M_{\texttt{lat}}(\phi(x_{h}),a_{h}),\phi\sharp M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs},h}(x_{h},a_{h})\right)\right],

(By definition of
\phi\sharp M^{\star}_{\texttt{obs}}
)

as desired. Summing over
h\in[H]
we obtain the desired bound. The bound
Eq. (
73
)

is a consequence of
Eq. (
72
)
and the data-processing inequality. Namely, using the definition of
\widetilde{M}^{\star,{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi,h}
from
Eq. (
70
)
and the joint convexity of the squared Hellinger distance we have:

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:getinnocuous}D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M_{\texttt{lat},h}(\cdot\mid s_{h},a_{h}),\widetilde{M}^{\star,{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi,h}(\cdot\mid s_{h},a_{h})\right)\leq\widetilde{\En}^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M_{\texttt{lat},h}(\cdot\mid s_{h},a_{h}),\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi,h}(\cdot\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})\right)\mid s_{h},a_{h}\right].

(77)

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\En^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M_{\texttt{lat},h}(\cdot\mid s_{h},a_{h}),\widetilde{M}^{\star,{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi,h}(\cdot\mid s_{h},a_{h})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\En^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}\left[\En^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M_{\texttt{lat},h}(\cdot\mid s_{h},a_{h}),\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi,h}(\cdot\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})\right)\mid s_{h},a_{h}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\En^{{\pi_{\texttt{lat}}}}_{\phi}\left[D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(M_{\texttt{lat},h}(\cdot\mid s_{h},a_{h}),\widetilde{M}^{\star}_{\phi,h}(\cdot\mid s_{1:h},a_{1:h})\right)\right],

as desired.
